The correct answer is B (Decubitus chest radiograph, symptomatic side down, to demonstrate layering along the dependent chest wall): A lateral decubitus film with the affected side down lets free pleural fluid shift to the dependent chest wall, and a layer thicker than about 1 cm confirms an effusion that is safe to sample. Fluid that does not move suggests loculation, which would call for ultrasound or CT guidance. Expiratory films are used for suspected small pneumothorax, not for effusion.
  3. The correct answer is B (Pump-handle motion, with the sternal ends rising and enlarging the front-to-back chest dimension): Ribs 1 to 5 rotate about an axis through the costovertebral and costotransverse joints, so the sternal end rises like a pump handle and the front-to-back diameter grows. Ribs 6 to 10 swing laterally like a bucket handle, and ribs 11 and 12 move as calipers. Diaphragmatic descent changes the vertical dimension, not the anterior chest wall rise felt here.
  4. The correct answer is C (Umbilical vein, returning blood directly from the placental villi toward the liver): The umbilical vein carries blood straight from the placenta and has the highest oxygen saturation, about 80 percent, in the fetal circuit. Every vessel downstream, including the inferior vena cava and descending aorta, carries blood already mixed with deoxygenated venous return. The umbilical arteries carry the least oxygenated blood back to the placenta.
  5. The correct answer is E (Upper trunk stretch injury of the brachial plexus at the fifth and sixth roots): Waiter's tip posture with a preserved grasp indicates an upper trunk (C5 to C6) stretch injury, which paralyzes deltoid, supraspinatus, infraspinatus, biceps and brachialis while sparing the hand. A lower trunk injury would produce a claw hand with absent grasp, and a total plexus injury would also abolish grasp.
  6. The correct answer is E (Progesterone from the corpus luteum, which resets the hypothalamic thermoregulatory set point upward after ovulation): After ovulation the corpus luteum secretes progesterone, which raises the hypothalamic set point and produces a biphasic temperature pattern. The rise ends at menses when the corpus luteum regresses, which fits a cycle without pregnancy. Human chorionic gonadotropin would sustain progesterone beyond day 28 only if implantation had occurred, and the luteinizing hormone surge lasts only about a day.
  7. The correct answer is C (Staphylococcus aureus, with Streptococcus pyogenes as a less frequent cause of the same lesions): Nonbullous impetigo shows honey-colored crusts from superficial infection, most often with Staphylococcus aureus today, though Streptococcus pyogenes remains a recognized cause. Group B streptococcus and Pseudomonas do not produce this pattern in healthy children.
  8. The correct answer is A (Defective ATP7B copper transporting ATPase impairing biliary copper excretion and ceruloplasmin incorporation): Neuropsychiatric symptoms, Kayser-Fleischer rings, low ceruloplasmin and hemolysis point to Wilson disease, an autosomal recessive ATP7B defect that blocks biliary copper excretion. Menkes disease involves ATP7A and causes copper deficiency, the opposite state.
  9. The correct answer is B (Cleavage of sialic acid that lets newly formed virions detach from the infected cell): Oseltamivir is a neuraminidase inhibitor. Neuraminidase cleaves terminal sialic acid, which frees new virions from the cell surface, so inhibition traps virus and limits spread. Amantadine blocks the M2 channel needed for uncoating, but it is no longer recommended because of resistance. Therapy works best when begun within 48 hours of symptom onset.
  10. The correct answer is C (Amiodarone, a class III agent with a lipophilic iodinated structure that accumulates in lung tissue): Amiodarone accumulates in tissues and causes pulmonary fibrosis, thyroid dysfunction, corneal deposits, hepatotoxicity, and blue-gray skin discoloration. Sotalol is associated with torsades, procainamide with lupus, flecainide with excess mortality after infarction, and lidocaine with neurotoxicity.
  11. The correct answer is E (Postmenopausal osteoporosis, reduced bone mass with normal mineralization and normal serum chemistries): A fragility fracture with diffusely low density and entirely normal calcium, phosphate, alkaline phosphatase, PTH and vitamin D fits primary osteoporosis, in which bone mass falls but mineralization is normal. Osteomalacia, hyperparathyroidism and Paget disease each alter at least one of these laboratory values.
  12. The correct answer is C (Residual insulin secretion suppresses lipolysis and ketogenesis but does not prevent severe hyperglycemia and osmotic diuresis): A small amount of insulin is enough to restrain adipose lipolysis and hepatic ketogenesis but not enough to control glucose uptake, so glucose climbs to extreme levels and causes osmotic diuresis, dehydration, and confusion. This is the hyperosmolar hyperglycemic state. Lactic acidosis from metformin would depress bicarbonate and pH.
  13. The correct answer is A (Acute tubular necrosis from ischemic injury, with sloughed tubular epithelial cells forming granular casts): Prolonged hypotension injures proximal tubular and thick ascending limb cells, which slough and form muddy brown granular casts. A fractional sodium excretion above 2 percent and no response to saline distinguish this from prerenal azotemia, which keeps a bland sediment and reverses with volume.
  14. The correct answer is D (Passive immunization with HBIG given together with starting the active vaccine series today): A nonimmune person exposed to a high risk source needs immediate antibodies from immune globulin plus active vaccination so that protection continues after the passive antibodies wane. Vaccination alone is too slow to protect during the incubation period. Antiretroviral drugs are used for HIV exposure and do nothing for this virus.
  15. The correct answer is C (Amiodarone, an iodinated class III agent with a long half-life and pulmonary fibrosis risk): Amiodarone has class I through IV actions, and its pulmonary toxicity (interstitial pneumonitis and fibrosis) is the classic dose-related adverse effect, so baseline and periodic lung testing is advised. Sotalol shares class III action but its signature toxicity is torsades de pointes, not lung fibrosis.
  16. The correct answer is C (Panacinar emphysema, with uniform destruction of the whole acinus from unopposed neutrophil elastase): PAS-positive, diastase-resistant globules in hepatocytes are misfolded alpha-1 antitrypsin, which indicates deficiency. Without this inhibitor, neutrophil elastase destroys the entire acinus evenly, producing panacinar emphysema that favors the lower lobes. Smoking-related centriacinar emphysema favors the upper lobes.
  17. The correct answer is B (Factitious thyrotoxicosis from exogenous hormone, with TSH suppression and shutdown of intrinsic gland synthesis): Low uptake with an undetectable thyroglobulin means the gland is not making or releasing its own hormone, so the hormone is coming from outside. This fits a supplement containing thyroid hormone. Thyroiditis also has low uptake but raises thyroglobulin as stored hormone leaks out.
  18. The correct answer is D (Central diabetes insipidus from impaired posterior pituitary release of antidiuretic hormone after surgery): Polyuria with high serum osmolality and dilute urine that concentrates after desmopressin shows a kidney that works but lacks the hormone. Pituitary surgery can injure the posterior pituitary or stalk and cause transient central diabetes insipidus. Nephrogenic disease would not respond to desmopressin.
  19. The correct answer is A (Down syndrome from nondisjunction, carrying an increased risk of cardiac and duodenal anomalies): The classic Down syndrome pattern on the quadruple screen is low alpha-fetoprotein and estriol with high hCG and inhibin A. Trisomy 18 lowers all four analytes, while an open neural tube defect raises alpha-fetoprotein. A screen is not diagnostic, so positive results lead to cell-free DNA or diagnostic testing.
  20. The correct answer is A (Document injuries, assess danger, offer safety planning and hotline resources, and respect her stated wishes): A capacitated adult victim retains the right to decide about police involvement when no law compels reporting, so the physician documents injuries, assesses lethality and offers a safety plan and resources. Overriding her wishes, involuntary admission, or contacting the abuser can increase danger and erode trust. Simply treating the fracture misses the chance to intervene.
  21. The correct answer is B (Complex regional pain syndrome type 1, a limb disorder with autonomic changes and intact nerves): Burning pain out of proportion to injury, allodynia, edema, temperature and sweating changes after a fracture define complex regional pain syndrome. Type 1 has no confirmed nerve lesion, as shown by normal conduction studies, whereas type 2 follows a defined nerve injury. Disuse atrophy lacks allodynia and autonomic features.
  22. The correct answer is A (Tibial nerve entrapment beneath the flexor retinaculum on the inner side of the ankle): A reproducible Tinel sign behind the medial malleolus with burning plantar paresthesias localizes the lesion to the tibial nerve beneath the flexor retinaculum, the tarsal tunnel. A normal Achilles reflex argues against S1 radiculopathy, and plantar fasciitis causes pain without sensory loss.
  23. The correct answer is D (Greater sarcomere stretch before contraction raises calcium sensitivity and increases the force generated): Frank-Starling states that greater end-diastolic stretch lengthens sarcomeres toward optimal overlap and increases troponin C calcium sensitivity, raising stroke volume with no neural input. Beta-1 stimulation also raises stroke volume but acts through cAMP and shifts the whole curve, which the stem excludes by stating there is no neural input.
  24. The correct answer is C (Deficient CD18 beta-2 integrin subunit, preventing firm neutrophil adhesion and transmigration into infected tissue): Neutrophilia with no neutrophils in tissue and delayed cord separation indicate leukocyte adhesion deficiency type 1, from defective CD18 so integrin-mediated firm adhesion and transmigration fail. Chronic granulomatous disease would produce tissue neutrophils with abscesses and granulomas, and Chediak-Higashi syndrome shows giant granules with partial albinism.
  25. The correct answer is C (Lactated Ringer 4800 mL in the first 8 hours, counted from the time of injury): The Parkland formula is 4 mL x weight (kg) x percent TBSA, which gives 9600 mL over 24 hours. Half of that, 4800 mL, is given in the first 8 hours measured from the time of the burn, not from hospital arrival, so an hour of the window has already passed. Giving the full volume early or using a smaller per-kilogram factor misapplies the formula.
  26. The correct answer is A (Inadequate caloric intake, which lowers weight first and spares length and head circumference initially): Caloric deprivation first decreases weight, then length, then head circumference, so isolated weight faltering with preserved length and head size points to insufficient intake, which is the most common cause of failure to thrive. Endocrine and genetic causes usually impair length early. Social stressors in the history support an intake problem rather than organic disease.
  27. The correct answer is D (All-trans retinoic acid, which overcomes the maturation block by acting on the abnormal fusion receptor): Auer rod-rich promyelocytes with t(15;17) and coagulopathy define acute promyelocytic leukemia, caused by the PML-RARA fusion that blocks differentiation. All-trans retinoic acid binds the fusion receptor and induces maturation, and it is started urgently because of the associated disseminated intravascular coagulation. Imatinib targets BCR-ABL and rituximab targets CD20.
  28. The correct answer is E (Common fibular nerve, wrapping the neck of the fibula just beneath the skin): The common fibular nerve is superficial as it winds around the fibular neck and is easily compressed by casts or straps. Its loss removes dorsiflexion and eversion, causing foot drop, with sensory loss on the dorsum, whereas a single branch lesion would spare either eversion or dorsiflexion.
  29. The correct answer is A (Olanzapine, whose histamine H1 and serotonin 5-HT2C antagonism promotes appetite and metabolic dysregulation): Marked weight gain, hypertriglyceridemia, and impaired fasting glucose with sedation fit olanzapine, whose H1 and 5-HT2C blockade drives appetite and insulin resistance. Haloperidol and aripiprazole cause movement side effects more than metabolic ones, and ziprasidone is more associated with QT prolongation.
  30. The correct answer is B (C6, because wrist extensors are intact while the C7 triceps are no longer functioning): The neurologic level is the lowest segment with normal motor function, and wrist extension (C6) is the lowest key muscle that is strong here. Triceps weakness shows C7 is not intact, and the intact lateral forearm and thumb sensation also fit C6. Naming the first absent myotome is a common error.
  31. The correct answer is C (Daily oral vitamin D from the first days of life to support calcium and phosphate absorption): Human milk contains little vitamin D, so exclusively breastfed infants need daily supplementation from early life to avoid rickets. The findings of metaphyseal cupping, widened wrists, and raised alkaline phosphatase and parathyroid hormone fit nutritional rickets. Iron prevents anemia and vitamin K prevents bleeding, but neither prevents rickets.
  32. The correct answer is E (Incretin receptor activation slowing gastric emptying and signaling hypothalamic centers to lower hunger): Semaglutide and related agents activate GLP-1 receptors, which slows gastric emptying, increases satiety through hypothalamic and brainstem circuits, and enhances glucose dependent insulin secretion. Nausea and early satiety follow from delayed gastric emptying. SGLT2 inhibitors cause modest weight loss through glycosuria, and orlistat acts on lipase, but neither is a weekly injectable.
  33. The correct answer is B (Intravenous lorazepam, then resume a long-acting benzodiazepine and taper it slowly over weeks): Abrupt cessation of a short-acting benzodiazepine causes a withdrawal syndrome with autonomic hyperactivity and seizures that is treated by restoring GABA-A agonism. A benzodiazepine stops the seizure and a long-acting agent allows a gradual taper. Flumazenil would worsen the problem, and phenytoin is ineffective for this seizure mechanism.
  34. The correct answer is D (Emergent surgical exploration with detorsion and bilateral orchiopexy rather than waiting for confirmatory imaging studies): Abrupt pain with a high riding transverse testis and no cremasteric reflex is testicular torsion, and testicular salvage falls steeply after about six hours. Surgery should not be delayed for imaging when suspicion is high, and the other testis is fixed because of the bell clapper deformity. Epididymitis has a gradual onset and a preserved reflex.
  35. The correct answer is C (Ejection fraction 50 percent with a cardiac index of 2.8 L/min/m2): Stroke volume is 140 minus 70, or 70 mL, so ejection fraction is 70 divided by 140, which is 50 percent. Cardiac output is 70 mL times 80 beats per minute, or 5.6 L/min, and dividing by 2.0 square meters gives a cardiac index of 2.8 L/min/m2.
  36. The correct answer is B (Uptake of the intrinsic factor cobalamin complex by cubilin receptors on distal small bowel enterocytes): Cobalamin bound to intrinsic factor is absorbed only at receptors in the terminal ileum, so ileal resection or disease causes deficiency even when gastric and pancreatic steps are normal. Pernicious anemia would impair intrinsic factor secretion, but her stomach is intact. Body stores last two to five years, which matches the delayed onset.
  37. The correct answer is A (Sensitivity of 90 percent, calculated as true positives divided by all culture-confirmed disease): The question asks what fraction of children with true infection the test detects, which is sensitivity: 90 true positives among 100 culture-positive children is 90 percent. Specificity, predictive values and overall accuracy answer different questions and depend on other cells of the table.
  38. The correct answer is D (Glucose oxidation and lipogenesis raise carbon dioxide production relative to oxygen consumption, straining respiratory muscles): Respiratory quotient is about 1.0 for carbohydrate, 0.8 for protein, and 0.7 for fat, so overfeeding glucose and converting it to fat (quotient above 1.0) increases carbon dioxide output per oxygen consumed. In a patient with COPD and limited reserve, the extra carbon dioxide raises required minute ventilation and precipitates hypercapnia. Fat has the lowest quotient, so shifting calories toward lipid is the usual adjustment.
  39. The correct answer is D (Psoas syndrome with hypertonic iliopsoas and type II lumbar dysfunction at the upper lumbar segments): A forward-bent stance, relief with hips flexed, a positive Thomas test, and deep paraumbilical tenderness with upper lumbar dysfunction describe psoas syndrome. Appendicitis can mimic it but is unlikely without fever or other abdominal findings, and the other options do not explain a hip flexion contracture.
  40. The correct answer is C (Right superior pubic shear, treated with muscle energy at the pubic symphysis): A higher right pubic tubercle indicates a superior pubic shear, and muscle energy uses the patient's isometric effort to correct it. An inferior shear would show a lower tubercle on that side. Counterstrain is passive, and a thrust across the sacroiliac joint does not address the pubic symphysis.
  41. The correct answer is E (CRAFFT, a six question tool built for teenagers that asks about riding with impaired drivers): CRAFFT was designed and validated for adolescents and is recommended for confidential screening in primary care. CAGE and AUDIT-C address alcohol in adult samples, and DAST-10 omits alcohol. PHQ-9 screens mood, not substance use.
  42. The correct answer is A (Rib raising along the thoracic costovertebral region, using gentle passive motion to modulate autonomic tone): Rib raising is a gentle, passive technique used in hospitalized pneumonia patients to reduce sympathetic tone and aid lymphatic and respiratory mechanics, and it carries little fracture risk. HVLA thrust is the tempting but inappropriate choice with osteoporosis and a prior compression fracture. The other techniques rest on autonomic claims that do not match lung physiology.
  43. The correct answer is A (Larger chamber size at fixed pressure raises tension on the myocardium, increasing energy use): Laplace's law gives wall tension proportional to pressure times radius divided by wall thickness, so a dilated chamber with unchanged thickness has higher tension per unit myocardium even at the same pressure, and tension is a major determinant of oxygen demand. Higher preload is present in regurgitation but does not address a question framed around Laplace.
  44. The correct answer is B (Secure the airway early, give intravenous broad spectrum antibiotics, and arrange surgical drainage with source control): Bilateral submandibular and sublingual swelling with floor of mouth elevation after a molar infection is Ludwig angina, a rapidly spreading cellulitis that can obstruct the airway. Airway protection comes first, followed by intravenous antibiotics covering oral flora and surgical drainage. Imaging in a patient with stridor risks catastrophic obstruction.
  45. The correct answer is C (Recall bias, because parents of ill infants remember and report past drug use more completely): Parents of ill infants search their memories harder, so they report past drug exposures more completely than control parents, producing recall bias that inflates the association. Selection bias concerns how controls were chosen, and confounding requires a third variable, which the stem does not describe. Lead-time bias belongs to screening studies, and interviewer knowledge is not mentioned.
  46. The correct answer is A (Depolarization of resting potential that inactivates sodium channels and reduces cell excitability): A higher extracellular potassium concentration lowers the potassium gradient, so the potassium equilibrium potential and resting potential become less negative. Sustained depolarization inactivates voltage-gated sodium channels through the inactivation gate, so fewer are available to open and excitability falls, which can lead to conduction block and arrhythmia. Hypokalemia produces hyperpolarization instead.
  47. The correct answer is E (IgA nephropathy with mesangial IgA deposition triggered by a mucosal infection): Recurrent gross hematuria beginning within one to two days of a mucosal infection, with normal complement, is typical of IgA nephropathy. Poststreptococcal disease follows an infection by one to three weeks and lowers C3. Thin basement membrane disease causes persistent microscopic hematuria rather than infection linked flares.
  48. The correct answer is D (Upper gastrointestinal reflex from esophageal and gastric irritation, with sympathetic segmental facilitation at T5 to T9): Meal-related burning, hoarseness, and nocturnal cough with normal imaging and spirometry suggest reflux, and the stomach and upper gastrointestinal tract refer to T5 to T9. The lungs refer to T1 to T6, the heart to T1 to T5, and the small bowel to T8 to T10, none of which fits both the symptoms and the levels as well.
  49. The correct answer is C (Start intravenous antibiotics without delay, then proceed to operative irrigation and surgical debridement): Early systemic antibiotics, ideally within the first hour, are the intervention most strongly linked to lower deep infection rates in open fractures, and urgent surgical debridement follows. Contaminated wounds are left open and not closed primarily. Waiting for culture results delays therapy, and routine wound cultures do not guide treatment.
  50. The correct answer is C (Palivizumab, a humanized monoclonal antibody directed against the F protein of this virus): This is RSV bronchiolitis in a premature infant. Palivizumab is a humanized monoclonal antibody against the RSV F protein, which mediates membrane fusion and syncytium formation, and it is given monthly during the season to high-risk infants. Ribavirin is an antiviral with limited use, and immune globulin and influenza vaccine do not provide RSV-specific protection.
  51. The correct answer is D (Reduced lysyl oxidase activity from copper deficiency, impairing collagen and elastin cross-linking): Sparse kinky hair, hypotonia, seizures and tortuous vessels in an infant with an affected maternal uncle indicate X-linked Menkes disease, in which defective copper transport lowers lysyl oxidase activity and cross-linking. Vitamin C deficiency and lysyl hydroxylase defects affect hydroxylation instead. Homocystinuria also produces lens dislocation and thrombosis rather than seizures with kinky hair.
  52. The correct answer is A (The mean exceeds the median, which exceeds the mode, because outliers drag the average): In a right-skewed distribution the tail of high values pulls the mean the farthest toward the right, the median sits in between, and the mode stays at the peak. A left-skewed pattern would reverse this order, which is the tempting error. The median resists outliers, so it moves much less than the mean.
  53. The correct answer is D (Soap and water washing with friction to physically remove spores from the hands): Clostridioides difficile forms spores that resist alcohol, so mechanical washing with soap and water is needed to lift them off the skin. Alcohol rubs work well against vegetative bacteria but not against spores, and repeating the application does not change that. Gloves are worn in addition to, not instead of, hand hygiene.
  54. The correct answer is C (Capitation shifts financial risk to the group, rewarding lower utilization and cost containment for each member): Capitation pays a fixed amount per enrolled person, so the group keeps the savings when it provides less care, which discourages extra referrals and tests. Fee for service pays for each service and promotes volume. Bundled payment prices an episode, and pay for performance rewards measured quality rather than a fixed per-member payment.
  55. The correct answer is D (Pressure changes that enhance lymph flow toward the thoracic duct and mobilize immune cells): The thoracic pump alters intrathoracic and abdominal pressures, which increases lymphatic return toward the thoracic duct and is thought to mobilize immune cells and clear inflammatory fluid. It does not kill bacteria directly, and rib motion, sympathetic, or vagal effects are not the primary rationale for this technique.
  56. The correct answer is C (Hepatic metastases release serotonin directly into the hepatic veins, bypassing first-pass degradation by hepatic monoamine oxidase): Serotonin from a midgut tumor drains into the portal vein, where monoamine oxidase in hepatocytes inactivates it to 5-hydroxyindoleacetic acid, so a small primary tumor is usually silent. Once metastases drain directly into the hepatic veins, serotonin reaches the systemic circulation and causes flushing, diarrhea, bronchospasm, and right-sided valvular fibrosis. Portal delivery alone does not produce the syndrome because the liver clears it.
  57. The correct answer is A (97 percent, the true negatives divided by every child who tested negative): Negative predictive value is true negatives divided by all negative results: 720 of 740, about 97 percent. Specificity is 720 of 800 (90 percent) because it conditions on children who truly lack infection, which is a different denominator. Positive predictive value (180 of 260, about 69 percent) concerns positive results, not negative ones.
  58. The correct answer is A (Ectopic ACTH secretion by a neuroendocrine lung tumor, resistant to glucocorticoid feedback suppression): A very high ACTH with pigmentation, hypokalemia, and no suppression after either dose of dexamethasone points to ectopic ACTH, most often small cell lung cancer. Pituitary adenomas usually suppress at least partly with the high dose. Adrenal sources have low ACTH.
  59. The correct answer is D (High risk with a score of seven; manage as presumed sleep apnea with opioid-sparing precautions): He scores one point each for snoring, tiredness, hypertension, body mass index over 35, age over 50, neck over 40 cm, and male sex, for a total of seven, which is high risk. Surgery need not be delayed for full testing, and the safe approach is to treat him as having presumed sleep apnea with opioid-sparing analgesia, monitoring, and CPAP available.
  60. The correct answer is D (Topical ketoconazole, an azole that inhibits ergosterol synthesis in Malassezia yeast): Greasy scale in sebum-rich areas that fluctuates with season and stress, especially in Parkinson disease, is seborrheic dermatitis, in which Malassezia yeast contributes to inflammation. Topical azoles such as ketoconazole inhibit fungal ergosterol synthesis and are first-line. Terbinafine and nystatin target other fungi, and mupirocin and permethrin target bacteria and mites.
  61. The correct answer is C (Low potassium and high bicarbonate, from collecting duct sodium reabsorption driving potassium and hydrogen secretion): Aldosterone increases epithelial sodium channel and sodium potassium ATPase activity in principal cells, so sodium is retained while potassium is secreted, and alpha intercalated cells secrete more hydrogen. The result is hypertension with hypokalemia and metabolic alkalosis. Low renin separates this from secondary causes such as renal artery stenosis.
  62. The correct answer is E (Partial estrogen agonist activity in uterine tissue, stimulating endometrial proliferation despite breast antagonism): Tamoxifen is a selective estrogen receptor modulator that antagonizes the receptor in breast tissue but acts as a partial agonist in the endometrium. This raises the risk of hyperplasia, polyps, and carcinoma, so postmenopausal bleeding on therapy needs evaluation. Aromatase inhibitors lower estrogen and do not stimulate the uterus.
  63. The correct answer is C (Blocking the dopamine and norepinephrine transporters, increasing synaptic catecholamine availability in prefrontal circuits): Methylphenidate blocks reuptake by the dopamine and norepinephrine transporters, which raises catecholamine signaling in prefrontal circuits and improves attention and impulse control. Atomoxetine acts on the norepinephrine transporter alone, and guanfacine acts through alpha-2A receptors, so they are the closest alternatives.
  64. The correct answer is D (Elevated plasma aldosterone with suppressed plasma renin activity, giving a high aldosterone to renin ratio): Hypertension with hypokalemia and metabolic alkalosis suggests primary aldosteronism. Autonomous aldosterone raises volume and suppresses renin, so the aldosterone to renin ratio is high. High renin with high aldosterone would be secondary, such as from renal artery stenosis.
  65. The correct answer is A (Decline kindly, explaining that dating a current patient is inappropriate, and continue her care professionally): Romantic involvement with a current patient exploits the power imbalance and is considered unethical, so he should decline kindly and keep treating her. Transferring care in order to date, or waiting a set period, does not remove that imbalance after a six year relationship. Referral is not needed when professional boundaries can simply be maintained.
  66. The correct answer is A (Intramuscular epinephrine into the lateral thigh to reverse vasodilation, edema and bronchospasm at once): Anaphylaxis with hypotension and airway involvement requires immediate intramuscular epinephrine, which acts on alpha and beta receptors to restore vascular tone and relieve bronchospasm. The other measures are adjuncts: antihistamines and steroids act too slowly, and fluids help only after the receptor effects are addressed.
  67. The correct answer is D (Dorsal root and posterior column degeneration abolishing proprioceptive input needed for balance): Tabes dorsalis of tertiary neurosyphilis causes degeneration of the dorsal roots and posterior columns, which removes position and vibration sense, abolishes stretch reflexes, and produces a sensory ataxia worse without visual compensation. Pupils that accommodate but do not react are the Argyll Robertson sign. Cobalamin deficiency also affects dorsal columns but gives hyperreflexia from corticospinal involvement, not absent reflexes.
  68. The correct answer is E (IgA vasculitis with small vessel immune complex deposition involving skin, joints, bowel, and kidney): Palpable purpura on the buttocks and legs with abdominal pain, arthritis, and hematuria in a child after an infection is IgA vasculitis, and the normal platelet count and clotting times show the rash is not from a bleeding disorder. Immune thrombocytopenia and hemolytic uremic syndrome both lower the platelet count, which the panel excludes.
  69. The correct answer is D (Improve rib cage excursion and reduce excess sympathetic tone to aid lymphatic and mucus clearance): Rib raising is an adjunct that improves chest wall motion and lowers sympathetic hyperactivity at the thoracic levels supplying the lungs, which supports lymphatic flow and secretion clearance. It does not treat the infection, and its levels are sympathetic rather than vagal.
  70. The correct answer is D (Howell-Jolly bodies, representing nuclear remnants no longer pitted out by splenic macrophages): The spleen removes nuclear remnants from red cells, so after splenectomy circulating Howell-Jolly bodies appear, along with mild thrombocytosis and target cells. Spherocytes and bite cells arise from splenic conditioning that no longer occurs. Schistocytes would require a microangiopathic process, and teardrop cells would suggest marrow fibrosis.
  71. The correct answer is B (L4 flexed, rotated left, sidebent left, a type II single segment dysfunction): One segment moving freely only into flexion with sidebending and rotation to the same side is a type II single segment dysfunction named for its free directions. A neutral group curve is excluded because neighbors move normally and no group appears in neutral. The sore throat is an unrelated detail.
  72. The correct answer is B (Immediately medial to the pulsation, in its own compartment of the sheath): From lateral to medial the femoral triangle holds the nerve, then the artery, then the vein, with the nerve outside the femoral sheath. The vein therefore lies immediately medial to the arterial pulse, and puncture above the inguinal ligament risks retroperitoneal bleeding.
  73. The correct answer is D (Adult children act as surrogates using her known wishes, with ethics consultation if they remain divided): With no spouse or directive, most state hierarchies place adult children next, and they should apply substituted judgment based on what she would have wanted. When they cannot agree, an ethics consultation is the usual next step before any court involvement. The physician does not take over the decision just because the family is divided.
  74. The correct answer is D (Intravenous calcium gluconate, raising the threshold potential and restoring membrane stability within minutes): ECG changes with a widened QRS signal imminent conduction failure, so membrane stabilization with intravenous calcium comes first because it acts within minutes but does not lower potassium. Insulin with dextrose and albuterol then shift potassium into cells over the following half hour, and dialysis removes it definitively.
  75. The correct answer is E (Cervical sympathetic chain at the stellate ganglion, which carries sympathetic fibers to the eye and face): A right apical tumor in a smoker is a superior sulcus (Pancoast) tumor. Involvement of the lower brachial plexus produces the ulnar distribution pain, while interruption of the stellate ganglion sympathetics causes ptosis, miosis, and anhidrosis, which is Horner syndrome. The brachial plexus explains the pain but not the eye findings.
  76. The correct answer is B (Acute viscerosomatic reflex from the pulmonary infection, with sympathetic segmental facilitation at that level): Warm, moist, edematous, erythematous tissue indicates an acute process, and its T2 to T6 location matches the segmental supply of the lung, so it reflects viscerosomatic facilitation from the new pneumonia. Chronic COPD would produce cool, fibrotic, ropy tissue, and a cardiac reflex would involve the heart's levels, not a right-sided lung pattern.
  77. The correct answer is C (Withhold the live vaccine now and give it postpartum before hospital discharge): Measles, mumps and rubella vaccine contains live attenuated virus and is not given during pregnancy because of a theoretical fetal risk. Nonimmune women are vaccinated after delivery, ideally before leaving the hospital. Immune globulin is not recommended for rubella prevention, and waiting for a later trimester does not remove the contraindication.
  78. The correct answer is D (Decreased systemic vascular resistance with normal or elevated cardiac output and low or normal wedge pressure): Early septic shock is distributive: cytokine driven vasodilation lowers resistance, and cardiac output is preserved or raised, producing warm skin and bounding pulses. Filling pressures are low or normal because of venous pooling and capillary leak. Cold extremities with a low output appear later in sepsis, and high resistance profiles belong to cardiogenic and hypovolemic states.
  79. The correct answer is B (Fluoxetine at a higher dose, an SSRI with evidence for reducing episodes of overeating and vomiting): Fluoxetine is the only medication approved for bulimia nervosa and is used at higher doses than for depression. Bupropion is contraindicated because purging and electrolyte disturbance lower the seizure threshold, a risk that is higher here given her seizure history. Lithium is unsafe when vomiting causes volume and sodium loss.
  80. The correct answer is C (Neglect, a caregiver's failure to meet needs for food, fluids, hygiene and pressure relief): Pressure ulcers, dehydration, poor hygiene and weight loss in a dependent person whose caregiver is responsible for her care indicate neglect. Control of finances alone could raise concern for exploitation, but the physical findings here are failures of care rather than misuse of money. Self-neglect applies when no caregiver exists, and absence of injuries argues against physical abuse.
  81. The correct answer is A (Structure and function are reciprocally interrelated, so altered framework mechanics can change organ behavior): The stem links a spinal mechanical problem to organ function, which is the reciprocal relationship between structure and function. The unity and self-regulation tenets describe other ideas, and the fourth tenet concerns how treatment is chosen. The diagnostic criteria for somatic dysfunction are a useful fact but are not one of the four tenets.
  82. The correct answer is C (Impaired neural crest migration into the first pharyngeal arch, disrupting jaw and ear development): Treacher Collins syndrome results from impaired neural crest migration into the first pharyngeal arch, producing mandibular and malar hypoplasia, ear malformation and conductive hearing loss. A third pouch defect would produce hypocalcemia and immune deficiency, which the normal calcium argues against. Second arch derivatives such as the stapes would not explain the small jaw.
  83. The correct answer is C (Indirect inguinal hernia following a persistent peritoneal outpouching along the path of testicular descent): A sac lateral to the inferior epigastric vessels that traverses the deep ring and enters the cord is an indirect hernia, arising from a patent processus vaginalis. A direct hernia lies medial to those vessels and pushes through the posterior wall. Femoral, Spigelian, and obturator hernias have different anatomy.
  84. The correct answer is A (Chapman reflex from appendiceal irritation; arrange urgent surgical evaluation and imaging, not OMT alone): A tender ropy nodule at the tip of the right twelfth rib is the classic anterior Chapman reflex for the appendix, and with migrating pain, anorexia, and fever it signals probable appendicitis. Chapman reflexes support diagnosis but never replace surgical evaluation and imaging, since delay risks perforation. Rib dysfunction or renal reflexes do not explain the migration pattern.
  85. The correct answer is E (Hypermethylation and silencing of the FMR1 promoter after an excessive CGG repeat expansion): Fragile X syndrome results from CGG repeat expansion in the 5 prime untranslated region of FMR1, causing promoter hypermethylation and loss of FMRP. The long face, large ears, macroorchidism and X-linked inheritance fit. The other repeat disorders involve different repeats, locations and mechanisms.
  86. The correct answer is E (Parasympathetic fibers from S2 to S4 relayed through the pelvic splanchnic nerves to the bladder): Detrusor contraction for voiding is parasympathetic, arising from S2 to S4 and carried by the pelvic splanchnic nerves. Sacral somatic dysfunction is osteopathically relevant because it lies near this outflow. The hypogastric sympathetic route promotes storage, and the pudendal nerve is somatic to the external sphincter.
  87. The correct answer is A (Columnar epithelium with goblet cells replacing the normal distal squamous lining): Chronic reflux produces intestinal metaplasia in which columnar epithelium with goblet cells replaces squamous mucosa (Barrett esophagus), and this is the precursor of esophageal adenocarcinoma through dysplasia. Basal zone hyperplasia is an ordinary reflux change without malignant potential, and viral inclusions suggest herpes esophagitis in immunocompromised patients.
  88. The correct answer is D (Intravenous oxytocin to stimulate myometrial contraction, the standard first-line uterotonic agent for atony): Uterine atony after prolonged labor and twin delivery is treated with fundal massage and oxytocin first. Carboprost is a prostaglandin F2 alpha analog that can cause bronchoconstriction, so asthma makes it a poor early choice, and ergots are second-line. Tranexamic acid is an adjunct, not a replacement for a uterotonic.
  89. The correct answer is B (Exposure and response prevention therapy combined with a high-dose SSRI such as fluoxetine): Obsessive-compulsive disorder responds best to exposure and response prevention, in which the patient faces the trigger without performing the ritual, plus an SSRI, which often needs higher doses and longer trials than for depression. Clomipramine is a second-line serotonergic drug, but noradrenergic tricyclics like desipramine lack this effect. Benzodiazepines do not treat obsessions.
  90. The correct answer is E (Palmar aponeurosis, where myofibroblast proliferation and collagen deposition shorten the fascia and flex the digit): Dupuytren contracture is a fibroproliferative disorder of the palmar aponeurosis, with myofibroblasts and thickened collagen forming nodules and cords that flex the ring and little fingers. The flexor tendons themselves are spared, which separates it from trigger finger. Risk factors include Northern European ancestry, diabetes and alcohol use.
  91. The correct answer is E (Extension into surrounding normal skin with little tendency to regress over time): Keloids grow beyond the original wound boundary, show thick hyalinized collagen bundles, and rarely regress. Hypertrophic scars stay within the wound and often improve over a year or two. The matrix in both scars is type I and III collagen, not type II.
  92. The correct answer is C (Absent hepatic UDP-glucuronosyltransferase activity that blocks bilirubin conjugation and cannot be improved by enzyme induction): Crigler-Najjar type I is complete loss of UGT1A1, so unconjugated bilirubin accumulates, crosses the immature blood-brain barrier and causes kernicterus, and induction fails because no enzyme exists to induce. Gilbert syndrome is the mild partial form and rarely reaches levels this high. Dubin-Johnson, Rotor syndrome and biliary atresia all raise conjugated bilirubin.
  93. The correct answer is E (Suboccipital inhibition, a soft tissue technique using sustained pressure to relax overactive posterior neck muscles): Sustained gentle pressure over the muscles at the base of the skull until the tissue relaxes is suboccipital inhibition, a soft tissue technique commonly used for tension-type headache. No patient contraction or thrust is involved, which excludes muscle energy and high velocity low amplitude, and the physician is not positioning a tender point as in counterstrain.
  94. The correct answer is A (Brush border enteropeptidase cleaving trypsinogen to trypsin, which then activates other pancreatic proenzymes): Enteropeptidase on the duodenal brush border cleaves trypsinogen to trypsin, which then activates chymotrypsinogen, proelastase and procarboxypeptidase. In hereditary pancreatitis premature or persistent intrapancreatic trypsin activity overwhelms the protective inhibitors and digests the gland.
  95. The correct answer is E (Right hemisection of the cord interrupting dorsal column, corticospinal and crossed spinothalamic fibers): A hemisection produces ipsilateral loss of vibration and proprioception (dorsal columns, crossed in the medulla) and ipsilateral upper motor neuron weakness (corticospinal tract, crossed in the medulla), but contralateral loss of pain and temperature because spinothalamic fibers cross within a few segments of entering. Here the right leg has weakness and dorsal column loss, and the left leg lost pain. Complete transection would affect both sides.
  96. The correct answer is D (Intramuscular benztropine, an antimuscarinic restoring balance between cholinergic and dopaminergic striatal activity): Young men given a high potency antipsychotic can develop acute dystonia within hours as dopamine blockade leaves cholinergic activity unopposed in the striatum. An anticholinergic such as benztropine or diphenhydramine rapidly relieves it, while propranolol treats akathisia and dantrolene or bromocriptine are used for hyperthermic syndromes.
  97. The correct answer is A (Structure and function are reciprocally interrelated, so altered tissue mechanics impair fluid movement): The reasoning links an altered tissue structure to impaired function, which is the reciprocal relationship between structure and function. Self-regulation also supports healing, but this plan targets a mechanical cause of the disordered fluid movement directly. The fourth tenet integrates the others rather than identifying the specific link.
  98. The correct answer is A (C3 to C5, whose anterior rami merge to form the phrenic nerve): The phrenic nerve arises from C3, C4, and C5, so cervical somatic dysfunction there can impair diaphragm motion. The saying is that C3, 4, 5 keeps the diaphragm alive. Lumbar levels mark where the crura attach but do not supply motor fibers.
  99. The correct answer is E (Start high dose glucocorticoids now and continue them while the biopsy is arranged): Headache, jaw claudication, scalp tenderness, a very high ESR, and transient vision loss indicate giant cell arteritis, where delayed treatment risks irreversible blindness. High dose glucocorticoids are started immediately because biopsy remains diagnostic for days to weeks after steroids begin. Steroid sparing agents are adjuncts, not initial therapy.
  100. The correct answer is C (Arrested alveolar and vascular growth leaving fewer, larger, simplified alveoli with only mild fibrosis): Bronchopulmonary dysplasia in the surfactant era follows injury to an immature lung and is dominated by arrested alveolar septation and abnormal vascular growth, giving fewer, larger alveoli with less fibrosis. The older form with severe airway necrosis and heavy fibrosis is now uncommon. Hyaline membranes belong to the acute phase.

Practice Test 6 Answer Key

  1. The correct answer is A (Blockade of voltage-gated sodium channels preventing action potential propagation along nerve axons): Tetrodotoxin in pufferfish binds the outer pore of voltage-gated sodium channels and blocks the rising phase of the action potential, causing paresthesias followed by flaccid paralysis with preserved consciousness. Botulinum toxin and curare act at the neuromuscular junction, and organophosphates would cause cholinergic excess with fasciculations and miosis.
  2. The correct answer is D (Muscle glycogen phosphorylase, preventing release of glucose 1-phosphate from stored glycogen): Exercise intolerance with a second wind, myoglobinuria, and a flat lactate but normal ammonia rise on forearm exercise indicates McArdle disease, deficiency of muscle glycogen phosphorylase. Muscle phosphofructokinase deficiency also blocks lactate rise, but it more often features hemolysis and no second wind. Normal ammonia rise argues against myoadenylate deaminase deficiency.
  3. The correct answer is D (She may consent to evaluation and treatment herself, and confidentiality applies as for adults): Most states allow minors to consent to diagnosis and treatment of sexually transmitted infections, and the visit remains confidential. This is a separate legal exception from the emergency exception, so no imminent danger is needed. Neither parental permission nor a court order is required.
  4. The correct answer is A (Ventilation is preserved while perfusion falls, so the ratio rises toward infinity as dead space): A pulmonary embolus blocks blood flow to a ventilated region, so the airways still receive gas but the capillaries do not receive blood. Perfusion approaches zero and V/Q approaches infinity, which is physiologic dead space. Low-ratio units and shunts arise from airway or alveolar filling problems, not vascular occlusion.
  5. The correct answer is D (Chronic viscerosomatic reflex from the descending colon with persistent sympathetic facilitation at the lumbar level): Cool, dry, ropy, fibrotic tissue indicates chronicity, and L1 to L2 is the sympathetic level of the descending colon, which fits constipation. Kidney and ureter share L1 but would not explain the bowel complaint. Acute reflexes would be warm and boggy, and sacral dysfunction alone does not account for the lumbar tissue changes.
  6. The correct answer is C (Terbinafine, which blocks squalene epoxidase and kills dermatophytes with little CYP3A4 drug interaction burden): Terbinafine is fungicidal against dermatophytes by inhibiting squalene epoxidase and has the highest cure rate for toenail onychomycosis. Itraconazole is effective but inhibits CYP3A4, a risk with simvastatin and amiodarone. Nystatin is poorly absorbed and acts on Candida rather than nail dermatophytes.
  7. The correct answer is B (Intravenous calcium gluconate to stabilize the myocyte membrane against potassium induced arrhythmia risk): Peaked T waves with a widened QRS show that hyperkalemia is already affecting conduction, so the membrane must be stabilized immediately with intravenous calcium. Insulin with dextrose and albuterol lower the level by shifting potassium, and they follow calcium. Polystyrene and furosemide remove potassium too slowly to be the first step.
  8. The correct answer is D (Fibrous adhesions from prior pelvic surgery kinking a loop of small bowel): Previous abdominal or pelvic surgery is the most common cause of small bowel obstruction in adults, because adhesions kink or trap bowel loops, and a collapsed colon confirms a high grade small bowel process. Femoral hernia is a classic miss in older women but the examination found no groin mass, and gallstone ileus requires air in the biliary tree.
  9. The correct answer is A (Short mean sleep latency with REM sleep appearing within fifteen minutes of sleep onset): Cataplexy with hypnagogic hallucinations and unrefreshed daytime sleepiness is narcolepsy type 1, caused by loss of hypocretin neurons. Testing shows a mean latency of eight minutes or less and sleep onset REM periods. Circadian delay would show normal architecture when sleep is allowed at the preferred time, and apnea would produce obstructive events.
  10. The correct answer is B (Intrapulmonary shunt, with perfused alveoli filled by exudate that cannot take up oxygen): Lobar pneumonia fills alveoli with exudate while their capillaries still carry blood, producing a right-to-left intrapulmonary shunt. Blood from these units cannot be oxygenated no matter how much oxygen is inspired, so the PaO2 barely improves. The low PaCO2 and tachypnea show that ventilation is not the problem.
  11. The correct answer is B (Factor VII has the shortest half-life of the vitamin K dependent proteins and falls first): Vitamin K carboxylates factors II, VII, IX, and X, and obstructive jaundice prevents bile-dependent absorption of this fat-soluble vitamin. Factor VII has a half-life of only a few hours, so the extrinsic pathway test prolongs before the intrinsic one. Protein C falls early too, but it affects thrombotic tendency rather than the prothrombin time, and a normal platelet count excludes a platelet cause.
  12. The correct answer is E (Accumulated deoxyadenosine triphosphate inhibiting ribonucleotide reductase, limiting deoxynucleotide supply for lymphocyte DNA synthesis): Adenosine deaminase deficiency causes deoxyadenosine to accumulate and be phosphorylated to dATP, which inhibits ribonucleotide reductase so lymphocytes cannot make deoxynucleotides for DNA synthesis. This affects T, B and natural killer cells and can produce rib cupping. Purine nucleoside phosphorylase deficiency also raises a deoxynucleotide but classically spares B cells.
  13. The correct answer is E (Ectopic secretion of antidiuretic hormone by the tumor cells, causing renal retention of free water): Hyponatremia with low serum osmolality, inappropriately concentrated urine, and euvolemia is the syndrome of inappropriate antidiuretic hormone secretion, which small cell carcinoma produces ectopically. Adrenal failure would cause volume depletion and hyperkalemia. Corticotropin secretion by the same tumor type produces hypokalemic alkalosis, not hyponatremia.
  14. The correct answer is C (Occipitoatlantal flexed, sidebent left and rotated right, named for the directions of greatest freedom): Somatic dysfunction is named by the direction of freedom, and the occipitoatlantal joint couples sidebending and rotation to opposite sides, as seen here with left sidebending and right rotation plus flexion. Naming by restriction reverses the label. The atlantoaxial joint is mostly rotation, and type II same-sided coupling applies to C2 through C7.
  15. The correct answer is E (Cholesterol 7-alpha-hydroxylase, the microsomal cytochrome P450 enzyme initiating the classic bile acid synthesis pathway): Cholesterol 7-alpha-hydroxylase is feedback inhibited by bile acids returning through the enterohepatic circulation, so sequestrants remove that inhibition and raise its activity. HMG-CoA reductase is tempting because it also rises, but it controls cholesterol formation, not bile acid formation.
  16. The correct answer is B (Neural crest cells that migrated ventrally and differentiated into catecholamine secreting chromaffin cells): Adrenal medullary chromaffin cells are modified postganglionic sympathetic neurons derived from neural crest, so pheochromocytoma arises from that lineage. The adrenal cortex, by contrast, comes from intermediate mesoderm and makes steroids, not catecholamines. Neural tube neuroectoderm forms central nervous system cells rather than peripheral crest derivatives.
  17. The correct answer is E (Defective mineralization of osteoid matrix because low calcium and phosphate prevent hydroxyapatite deposition on collagen): Low vitamin D with low calcium and phosphate, high alkaline phosphatase, proximal weakness, and pseudofractures indicates adult osteomalacia: osteoid is laid down normally but cannot mineralize. Osteoporosis preserves mineralization, Paget disease is focal with normal calcium, and hyperparathyroidism raises calcium.
  18. The correct answer is B (Repetitive acceleration and deceleration with rotation that tears bridging veins crossing from cortex to sinus): Bilateral retinal hemorrhages with subdural collections of different ages and no matching history indicate abusive head trauma. Violent shaking produces rotational acceleration and deceleration that shears bridging veins. Epidural bleeds arise from arterial tears with skull fracture and are usually single and lens shaped.
  19. The correct answer is C (Autonomous gastrin secretion from a neuroendocrine tumor causing sustained parietal cell hyperstimulation): A marked fasting gastrin with very acidic gastric pH and a paradoxical rise after secretin identifies a gastrinoma, since normal G cells and G-cell hyperplasia do not increase gastrin with secretin. Atrophic gastritis and acid suppression also raise gastrin, but only with a high gastric pH.
  20. The correct answer is D (Hydroxylation of proline and lysine residues within the endoplasmic reticulum): The gum bleeding, perifollicular hemorrhages and corkscrew hairs indicate scurvy. Ascorbic acid is a cofactor for prolyl and lysyl hydroxylases, so without it procollagen cannot form a stable triple helix. Lysyl oxidase cross-linking depends on copper, and propeptide cleavage occurs outside the cell without vitamin C.
  21. The correct answer is D (Structure and function are reciprocally interrelated, so altered anatomy changes physiology and the reverse): The physician describes posture affecting breathing and circulation, and those functional changes then maintaining the posture, which is the two-way link between structure and function. The body unit tenet is true but would be about the whole person, and the fourth tenet concerns how treatment is chosen.
  22. The correct answer is A (Give anti-D antibody now, then again after delivery if the newborn is Rh positive): A negative indirect antiglobulin test means she is unsensitized, which is exactly when prophylaxis works. Anti-D given at 28 weeks protects against silent fetomaternal hemorrhage during the third trimester, and a second dose follows delivery when the newborn is Rh positive. Doppler surveillance applies to women who already carry anti-D antibodies, and waiting for a positive screen means sensitization has already occurred.
  23. The correct answer is A (Alginate exopolysaccharide forming a biofilm matrix that impairs phagocytosis and limits antibiotic penetration): Mucoid Pseudomonas aeruginosa in cystic fibrosis overproduces alginate, which builds a biofilm that shields bacteria from phagocytes and antibiotics. Exotoxin A and the type III secretion system are real virulence factors, but they act mainly in acute infection and tissue injury rather than chronic persistence. Protein A belongs to Staphylococcus aureus.
  24. The correct answer is B (Retinacular branches of the medial femoral circumflex artery running along the femoral neck): In adults the femoral head depends mainly on retinacular branches of the medial femoral circumflex artery that ascend along the neck, so a displaced subcapital fracture tears them. The artery of the ligamentum teres contributes little after childhood, which makes it a tempting but inadequate collateral source here.
  25. The correct answer is E (Epithelial stem cells in the bulge region of hair follicles and sweat gland ducts): When most of the epidermis is lost, regeneration comes from epithelial stem cells that persist in the bulge of hair follicles and in sweat gland ducts deep in the dermis. Merkel cells, Langerhans cells and melanocytes are specialized epidermal cells, and fibroblasts do not become keratinocytes.
  26. The correct answer is C (Frameshift deletion causing a premature stop codon and almost no dystrophin protein): Progressive proximal weakness with a Gowers maneuver, calf pseudohypertrophy and a creatine kinase in the tens of thousands in a young boy indicates Duchenne muscular dystrophy. Frameshift deletions truncate dystrophin and leave almost none, whereas in-frame deletions in Becker disease yield partially functional protein and a later, milder course. Myotonic dystrophy has a CTG expansion and shows myotonia.
  27. The correct answer is D (Retained uremic toxins impair platelet adhesion and aggregation despite a normal circulating platelet number): Uremia causes a qualitative platelet defect with poor adhesion and aggregation, so bleeding time is prolonged while platelet count and coagulation studies stay normal. Dialysis improves it, and desmopressin can be used for acute bleeding. An inherited receptor defect would have produced bleeding long before kidney disease.
  28. The correct answer is B (Left-to-right atrial shunting raising right ventricular volume and delaying pulmonic valve closure in both phases): An atrial septal defect equalizes atrial pressures through respiration, so right ventricular volume stays increased and pulmonic closure is delayed in both inspiration and expiration. This produces a fixed split. Bundle branch block and aortic delay cause paradoxical splitting, and mitral regurgitation does not fix the split.
  29. The correct answer is B (The terminal hair follicle infundibulum, followed by rupture and inflammation involving adjacent apocrine tissue): Hidradenitis suppurativa begins with follicular occlusion at the terminal hair follicle infundibulum, followed by rupture and a largely sterile inflammatory response in intertriginous skin. Obesity, smoking and female sex are risk factors. Sebaceous duct plugging defines acne, and cultures are usually unrevealing because infection is secondary.
  30. The correct answer is E (25 patients, calculated as the reciprocal of the 4 percentage point absolute risk difference): Absolute risk reduction is 10 percent minus 6 percent, or 4 percentage points (0.04), and number needed to treat is its reciprocal, 25. The relative risk reduction of 40 percent and the single event rates are not the correct denominators. Reporting 25 patients means about one recurrence is prevented for every 25 treated for a year.
  31. The correct answer is C (Anterior interosseous nerve, a motor branch of the median nerve in the forearm): The anterior interosseous nerve supplies flexor pollicis longus, the lateral half of flexor digitorum profundus and pronator quadratus, so injury abolishes the O sign without sensory loss because it has no cutaneous branch. Injury to the median trunk would add numbness of the lateral palm and weakness of pronation and wrist flexion.
  32. The correct answer is E (Urgent surgical evaluation with imaging, since viscerosomatic findings reflect disease and do not replace care): Migrating pain, anorexia, fever, and rebound tenderness indicate probable appendicitis, a surgical emergency. The paraspinal changes at T10 to T11 are a viscerosomatic reflex of the disease and do not justify manipulative treatment in place of urgent evaluation.
  33. The correct answer is B (Circulating exfoliative toxins cleave desmoglein 1 in the granular layer of the epidermis): Staphylococcal scalded skin syndrome results from exfoliative toxins cleaving desmoglein 1 in the stratum granulosum, so sloughing is superficial and the mucosa is spared because mucosal desmoglein 3 compensates. Drug induced cytotoxic injury would involve the mucosa and full thickness epidermis.
  34. The correct answer is D (Precontemplation, with no intention to change behavior and little recognition that a problem exists): Precontemplation describes a person with no intention to change in the next six months, often with denial of the problem, as here. Contemplation involves recognizing the problem and weighing change. Preparation, action and maintenance all require a plan or active behavior change that she lacks.
  35. The correct answer is B (Risk-reducing bilateral salpingo-oophorectomy, since surveillance tests cannot reliably detect early tumors in carriers): BRCA1 carriers have a high lifetime risk of ovarian and fallopian tube cancer, and no screening strategy has been shown to detect it early enough to save lives. After childbearing is complete, surgical removal of the tubes and ovaries is advised. Mastectomy lowers breast risk only, and tamoxifen does not protect the ovary.
  36. The correct answer is E (Mitochondrial beta-oxidation of medium-chain fatty acids, which limits ketone production and gluconeogenic support during fasting): Hypoketotic hypoglycemia with elevated octanoylcarnitine identifies medium-chain acyl-CoA dehydrogenase deficiency. Without beta-oxidation the liver cannot generate ketones or the energy for gluconeogenesis during fasting. Defects of glycogen or gluconeogenesis cause lactic acidosis, and ketone use defects cause ketosis.
  37. The correct answer is D (Coagulase-negative staphylococcus, which forms biofilm on prosthetic material and causes low-virulence delayed infection): Delayed prosthetic joint infection with a low-grade course and slime production is classically caused by coagulase-negative staphylococci that adhere to hardware in a biofilm. Staphylococcus aureus tends to cause an earlier, more fulminant infection. Cutibacterium acnes is typical of shoulder arthroplasty, and tuberculosis would be unusual and granulomatous.
  38. The correct answer is A (T5 to T9 paraspinal segments, matching the sympathetic supply of stomach and duodenum): Stomach and duodenal sympathetic fibers arise from T5 to T9, so visceral irritation facilitates the matching paraspinal segments and produces warm, boggy, tender tissue early in the process. The upper levels of T1 to T4 are tempting because the pain is in the upper abdomen, but they supply the head and neck. Colonic and bladder levels lie lower in the thoracolumbar cord.
  39. The correct answer is E (Prostaglandin E1 infusion to maintain ductal patency and permit mixing between parallel circulations): Early cyanosis with an egg-on-a-string silhouette indicates transposition of the great arteries, where pulmonary and systemic circuits run in parallel. Survival depends on a patent ductus or septal defect for mixing, so prostaglandin E1 is started. Closing the duct or relying on oxygen would worsen hypoxemia.
  40. The correct answer is C (Primary hyperparathyroidism from autonomous PTH secretion by a parathyroid adenoma, raising calcium and lowering phosphate): High calcium with low phosphate and an inappropriately elevated intact PTH indicates autonomous parathyroid secretion, and the hypercalciuria, stones, and subperiosteal resorption support it. Familial hypocalciuric hypercalcemia has low urinary calcium, malignancy suppresses PTH, and secondary disease occurs with low or normal calcium.
  41. The correct answer is C (Axillary nerve, stretched as it wraps the surgical neck of the humerus): The axillary nerve (C5, C6) hugs the surgical neck of the humerus and is stretched by an anterior glenohumeral dislocation, paralyzing deltoid and teres minor and leaving a patch of numbness over the lateral shoulder. Suprascapular injury weakens the start of abduction and external rotation but spares skin sensation, so it cannot explain the numbness.
  42. The correct answer is A (Antibodies against streptococcal M protein cross-reacting with cardiac myosin and valvular glycoproteins): Acute rheumatic fever is a type II hypersensitivity in which antibodies and T cells directed at streptococcal M protein cross-react with cardiac myosin and valve proteins through molecular mimicry. The picture shows a granulomatous focus typical of the carditis, not bacterial invasion. Immune complex and superantigen mechanisms explain different streptococcal syndromes.
  43. The correct answer is D (Case-control study, which starts from outcome status and then looks backward for earlier exposures): Starting with affected and unaffected children and looking back at exposure defines a case-control study, which suits rare outcomes. A cohort design would begin with exposure groups and follow them, and a trial assigns the exposure. A cross-sectional survey would capture too few cases of a rare tumor.
  44. The correct answer is D (Sensitized CD4 T helper cells releasing interferon gamma, which activates macrophages at the dermal site): The tuberculin reaction is a type IV delayed hypersensitivity driven by memory CD4 T cells that release interferon gamma and recruit and activate macrophages, peaking at 48 to 72 hours. The induration reflects cellular infiltration, not antibody or immune complexes. A positive result in a patient with a normal radiograph indicates latent infection or prior sensitization, not active disease.
  45. The correct answer is E (Rapid jejunal delivery of hyperosmolar chyme drawing fluid into the lumen and releasing vasoactive peptides): Bypassing the pylorus lets hyperosmolar food enter the jejunum quickly, drawing fluid into the bowel and releasing vasoactive peptides, which causes early dumping with palpitations, flushing, cramps, and diarrhea within 30 minutes. Late dumping occurs one to three hours after meals from reactive insulin surges and low glucose, which does not match her normal glucose. Ulcer and stricture produce pain or vomiting without this systemic pattern.
  46. The correct answer is E (Bicuspid aortic valve with possible later stenosis or regurgitation of the aortic outflow): Upper extremity hypertension with weak femoral pulses and rib notching from collateral intercostal arteries indicates coarctation of the aorta. A bicuspid aortic valve is the most common coexisting cardiac anomaly. The other lesions are right sided or cyanotic and are not typical companions.
  47. The correct answer is B (Late left ventricular depolarization delays aortic valve closure until after pulmonic valve closure): Left bundle branch block delays left ventricular depolarization, so aortic closure occurs after pulmonic closure, producing paradoxical splitting that is widest on expiration and disappears on inspiration when pulmonic closure is delayed. Physiologic splitting is the opposite pattern, widening on inspiration.
  48. The correct answer is E (Neisseria meningitidis spread by respiratory droplets among crowded young adults): Fever, meningismus, and a petechial rash in a dormitory resident indicate meningococcal meningitis with meningococcemia. The fluid shows markedly elevated pressure, neutrophilic pleocytosis, high protein, and low glucose, a bacterial pattern. Enterovirus gives lymphocytes with normal glucose, and tuberculosis is subacute with a lymphocytic picture.
  49. The correct answer is B (Halt the unit at once, keep access open with saline, and verify patient and unit labels): Fever, flank pain, hypotension and hemoglobinuria within minutes of starting a unit indicate acute hemolytic transfusion reaction from ABO incompatibility, usually a clerical error. The transfusion must stop immediately, intravenous access maintained with saline, and the labels and patient identity rechecked. Allergy or fever treatments and continuing the unit would allow more hemolysis.
  50. The correct answer is B (SGLT2 inhibitor, blocking proximal tubular glucose reabsorption and producing glucosuria with osmotic natriuresis): SGLT2 inhibitors block glucose reabsorption in the proximal tubule, lower glucose, and reduce heart failure hospitalization and chronic kidney disease progression through natriuresis and hemodynamic effects. Thiazolidinediones worsen fluid retention, and sulfonylureas and DPP-4 inhibitors lack this cardiorenal benefit. Metformin remains useful for glycemia but does not provide these specific outcomes.
  51. The correct answer is D (Inhibition of CYP3A4 by the macrolide raising statin plasma concentration and myotoxicity): Clarithromycin is a strong CYP3A4 inhibitor, and simvastatin depends heavily on that enzyme for clearance, so statin levels rise and cause rhabdomyolysis. An inducer would lower statin exposure and reduce the effect. Hepatic oxidation, not renal secretion, is the main route of elimination for this statin.
  52. The correct answer is A (Flow drops to one sixteenth, since resistance rises with the inverse fourth power of radius): Poiseuille's law gives resistance proportional to viscosity times length divided by radius to the fourth power. Halving the radius multiplies resistance by 16, so flow at constant driving pressure falls to one sixteenth. This steep dependence is why modest bronchoconstriction causes severe obstruction.
  53. The correct answer is A (Mobitz type I second-degree block with progressive decremental conduction within the atrioventricular node): Progressive PR lengthening followed by a nonconducted P wave defines Mobitz type I, which arises in the AV node and is common in athletes with high vagal tone. Mobitz type II shows a constant PR interval before an abrupt dropped beat and signals infranodal disease, often with a wide QRS.
  54. The correct answer is C (Lorazepam, because conjugation spares oxidative metabolism and prevents active metabolite accumulation in hepatic dysfunction): Alcohol withdrawal with hallucinations and autonomic hyperactivity is treated with benzodiazepines. In severe liver disease, lorazepam, oxazepam and temazepam are preferred because they are glucuronidated and lack active metabolites. Diazepam and chlordiazepoxide rely on hepatic oxidation and can accumulate into oversedation, and phenytoin does not treat withdrawal.
  55. The correct answer is C (Diltiazem, a nondihydropyridine blocker that relaxes coronary smooth muscle and prevents vasospasm): Rest pain at night with transient ST elevation and no fixed stenosis indicates vasospastic Prinzmetal angina. Calcium channel blockers and nitrates relax coronary smooth muscle and are first-line prevention. Nonselective beta blockers can leave alpha mediated vasoconstriction unopposed, and antiplatelet agents do not address spasm.
  56. The correct answer is B (Air or contrast enema, serving as both confirmation and nonoperative reduction): Colicky pain with lethargy and currant jelly stool is classic for intussusception, usually ileocolic. In a stable child without peritonitis, an enema is diagnostic and therapeutic. Surgery is reserved for failed reduction, perforation, or peritoneal signs.
  57. The correct answer is B (Interferon-gamma release assay using antigens such as ESAT-6 absent from the vaccine strain): BCG vaccination can cause a false positive tuberculin skin test, whereas interferon-gamma release assays use antigens that the vaccine strain lacks. With no symptoms and a normal radiograph, active disease is not suspected, so sputum studies and more imaging add little. Treating on the skin test alone would risk committing a vaccinated person to months of therapy without confirmation.
  58. The correct answer is C (Tibial nerve, addressed by myofascial release of the flexor retinaculum and articulatory subtalar mobilization): Burning plantar pain with tingling reproduced behind the medial malleolus indicates tibial nerve entrapment in the tarsal tunnel. Releasing the flexor retinaculum and mobilizing the subtalar joint addresses the mechanical cause. The peroneal, sural and saphenous nerves do not travel through that tunnel.
  59. The correct answer is E (Median nerve compression in the carpal tunnel, treated by releasing retinaculum and carpal restrictions): Sparing of the thenar skin places the lesion distal to the origin of the palmar cutaneous branch, in the carpal tunnel. Pronator syndrome compresses the same nerve more proximally and usually reduces thenar skin sensation. Guyon canal, cervical and thoracic outlet lesions follow different distributions. Releasing the retinaculum and carpal restrictions widens the tunnel.
  60. The correct answer is E (Warn the identified person and law enforcement, and arrange emergency involuntary psychiatric evaluation): A serious, specific threat against an identifiable person overrides confidentiality, and the duty to protect is met by warning the potential victim and notifying police while arranging safe evaluation. Privilege and consent requirements yield when danger is imminent. Waiting for a committee or scheduling a routine visit would leave the victim exposed.
  61. The correct answer is A (Angiotensin II-driven aldosterone secretion from an intact zona glomerulosa remains preserved despite ACTH suppression): Long term exogenous steroid suppresses ACTH and atrophies the zona fasciculata, causing secondary adrenal insufficiency with cortisol deficiency. Aldosterone is controlled mainly by angiotensin II and potassium, so the zona glomerulosa keeps working and potassium stays normal. Prednisone has little mineralocorticoid activity.
  62. The correct answer is C (Andrew Taylor Still, who opened the first college of osteopathy in Kirksville in 1892): Andrew Taylor Still founded osteopathic medicine and opened the American School of Osteopathy in Kirksville in 1892. Palmer began chiropractic in 1895 and Sutherland, Mitchell, and Frymann were later osteopathic physicians who developed specific methods.
  63. The correct answer is E (Normal anion gap metabolic acidosis with appropriate respiratory compensation from bicarbonate loss in stool): The anion gap is 138 minus 116 minus 11, or 11 mEq/L, which is normal, so the acidosis comes from bicarbonate loss in diarrheal stool with chloride retention. The Winter formula predicts a carbon dioxide of about 24.5 plus or minus 2 mm Hg, matching the measured 24, so compensation is appropriate. A lower or higher value would signal a second respiratory disorder.
  64. The correct answer is D (Elevated follicle-stimulating hormone with low estradiol and low inhibin B reflecting depletion of ovarian follicles): Menopause follows follicular depletion, so estradiol and inhibin B fall and the loss of negative feedback raises follicle-stimulating hormone. Hypothalamic amenorrhea shares low estradiol but with low gonadotropins. Atrophic vaginal tissue also argues against an estrogen-secreting tumor.
  65. The correct answer is B (Gamma chains bind 2,3-BPG poorly, raising oxygen affinity and shifting the curve leftward): Hemoglobin F has gamma chains in place of beta chains, and these bind 2,3-BPG weakly. Less 2,3-BPG binding stabilizes the relaxed form, increasing oxygen affinity and shifting the dissociation curve left, which favors oxygen transfer across the placenta. Hemoglobin F still binds oxygen cooperatively and carries four heme groups.
  66. The correct answer is A (Low chloride, low potassium, and elevated bicarbonate from loss of gastric hydrochloric acid): Repeated loss of gastric acid produces hypochloremic, hypokalemic metabolic alkalosis as the kidney retains bicarbonate and wastes potassium and hydrogen ions. Adrenal salt wasting causes hyperkalemia and acidosis, and diarrhea causes a nongap acidosis. Rehydration with saline and potassium should precede pyloromyotomy.
  67. The correct answer is E (Soft tissue and inhibition work with the head kept neutral, avoiding terminal neck positions): Dizziness and diplopia with head rotation suggest vertebrobasilar insufficiency, an absolute contraindication to cervical HVLA and a reason to avoid sustained rotation or extension. Techniques that keep the neck neutral, such as soft tissue and inhibition, treat the dysfunction without provoking vertebral artery compromise.
  68. The correct answer is A (Chylomicron accumulation from deficient lipoprotein lipase activity, preventing hydrolysis of triglycerides carried in chylomicrons): A floating cream layer over clear plasma means chylomicrons are accumulating, and with eruptive xanthomas, lipemia retinalis and pancreatitis-type pain this indicates lipoprotein lipase or apolipoprotein C-II deficiency. VLDL excess produces uniform turbidity, remnant disease raises cholesterol too, and the low LDL excludes familial hypercholesterolemia.
  69. The correct answer is C (Patent air-filled bronchi outlined by surrounding alveoli filled with inflammatory exudate): Air bronchograms appear when alveoli fill with exudate, which removes the normal air contrast and silhouettes the still-aerated bronchi. This is the radiographic hallmark of lobar pneumonia, and here it fits the clinical picture of pneumococcal infection. Bronchiectasis produces dilated, thick-walled airways, but those are seen in chronically scarred lung rather than in acute lobar consolidation.
  70. The correct answer is A (Act: adopt the checklist as standard practice and continue monitoring to confirm the gain is sustained): In the PDSA cycle, once the Study step shows the predicted, sustained improvement without harm, the Act step follows: adopt the change as standard work and keep measuring. Further Do cycles or rollout are appropriate when results are mixed or the change needs adaptation. Returning to Plan or collecting baseline data repeats completed steps.
  71. The correct answer is B (Ketamine, which preserves sympathetic tone and relaxes bronchial smooth muscle through catecholamine release): Ketamine stimulates sympathetic outflow and is a bronchodilator, which suits hypotension with active bronchospasm.
