The correct answer is E (Acute somatic dysfunction with local inflammatory vasodilation and edema from recent tissue injury at that segment): Acute somatic dysfunction presents with warm, moist, edematous, erythematous tissue, reflecting recent injury and local vasodilation. Chronic dysfunction becomes cool, dry and ropy with fibrosis. A viscerosomatic reflex would need a plausible organ source, which the history does not offer.
  58. The correct answer is D (Intravenous calcium gluconate to stabilize the cardiac myocyte membrane and antagonize potassium induced depolarization): Conduction changes on the ECG signal imminent arrhythmia, so the first step is to raise the threshold potential with calcium, which acts within minutes but does not lower potassium. Insulin with glucose, albuterol, and later dialysis then remove or shift the potassium. A resin or loop diuretic is too slow or ineffective in anuric kidney failure.
  59. The correct answer is E (Ability to understand relevant information, appreciate consequences, reason about options, and express a consistent choice): Capacity is decision specific and rests on understanding, appreciation, reasoning, and a stable expressed choice. It does not depend on whether the physician or family agrees with the decision, and a screening score or a psychiatric diagnosis alone does not settle it. Here the stated reasoning meets each element.
  60. The correct answer is B (Emergent surgical exploration and debridement alongside broad spectrum intravenous antibiotics and aggressive fluid resuscitation): Pain out of proportion, crepitus, bullae with gray fluid and systemic toxicity indicate necrotizing fasciitis, a surgical emergency in which delay raises mortality. Imaging and observation waste time, and antibiotics alone cannot reach necrotic tissue.
  61. The correct answer is B (Oral doxycycline for ten days, started on clinical diagnosis without waiting for serologic testing): Erythema migrans in an endemic area is diagnosed clinically, and serology is often negative this early, so doxycycline for ten days begins immediately. Intravenous therapy is reserved for carditis or meningitis, and observation risks dissemination.
  62. The correct answer is D (Counterstrain, passively positioning the patient into ease and holding about ninety seconds without a thrust): An INR above the usual target makes forceful or thrust techniques risky because of bleeding into soft tissue, so a gentle indirect technique such as counterstrain is the safest way to treat the tender segments. Muscle energy can be gentle in some forms, but the maximal contraction against rigid resistance described here adds avoidable strain.
  63. The correct answer is A (Urgent imaging and surgical consultation for suspected gastric volvulus, deferring visceral osteopathic treatment): Sudden pain, retching without vomiting, and inability to swallow suggest an obstructed or volvulized paraesophageal hernia, a surgical emergency. Visceral manipulation is contraindicated when strangulation or obstruction is suspected. Imaging and surgical consultation come first, and gentle diaphragm techniques do not replace them.
  64. The correct answer is A (Supraspinatus tendon with its subacromial bursa, pinched under the coracoacromial arch as the humerus elevates): The painful arc with positive Neer and Hawkins signs reflects subacromial impingement, in which the supraspinatus tendon and bursa are compressed beneath the coracoacromial arch. Normal external rotation strength argues against an infraspinatus problem. Labral pathology produces apprehension rather than pain on these maneuvers.
  65. The correct answer is B (Seborrheic keratosis, a benign epidermal proliferation of basaloid keratinocytes with horn cysts): Numerous stable, waxy, stuck-on brown plaques with a verrucous surface in an older adult are typical of seborrheic keratosis, a benign epidermal tumor with keratin-filled horn cysts. Melanoma would show change, asymmetry and color variation. Actinic keratoses are rough, scaly and erythematous rather than greasy and pasted on.
  66. The correct answer is C (Reduced plasma butyrylcholinesterase activity from an inherited variant, slowing hydrolysis of the depolarizing blocker): Succinylcholine is cleared by plasma butyrylcholinesterase, so an inherited atypical enzyme variant prolongs the block for hours and can run in families. A single standard dose does not usually produce phase II block, and malignant hyperthermia would present with rigidity, hypercarbia, and fever rather than isolated paralysis.
  67. The correct answer is B (Reduce mean arterial pressure by no more than about 25 percent over the first hour): Encephalopathy with papilledema is a hypertensive emergency, and cerebral autoregulation is reset to higher pressures. Lowering mean pressure by more than about 25 percent in the first hour risks watershed ischemia, so reduction is gradual toward the target over hours to days. Aortic dissection is an exception that needs faster control.
  68. The correct answer is A (Failed migration of GnRH-secreting neurons from the olfactory placode into the hypothalamus during fetal development): Low testosterone with low LH and FSH indicates hypogonadotropic hypogonadism, and anosmia identifies Kallmann syndrome. GnRH neurons arise in the olfactory placode and fail to migrate. Primary testicular failure would raise gonadotropins, and normal prolactin and imaging argue against a pituitary lesion.
  69. The correct answer is D (Medullary carcinoma arising from parafollicular C cells with amyloid deposits derived from calcitonin): Amyloid derived from calcitonin, with sheets of spindle cells and a family history of pheochromocytoma, indicates medullary carcinoma from C cells, part of MEN 2 with RET mutation. Diarrhea comes from secreted peptides. Papillary and follicular cancers arise from follicular cells and do not form amyloid.
  70. The correct answer is E (Glioblastoma, an IDH-wildtype astrocytic tumor with central necrosis and rapid growth across white matter): An irregular ring-enhancing mass with central necrosis that infiltrates across the corpus callosum in a butterfly pattern in an older adult is glioblastoma. Metastasis is the main competitor but is usually multiple and well circumscribed at the gray white junction, and an abscess is unlikely without fever or an infectious source.
  71. The correct answer is A (MEN1 gene loss of function, removing menin mediated tumor suppression in multiple endocrine glands): The triad of parathyroid hyperplasia, gastrin producing pancreatic islet tumor (Zollinger-Ellison physiology), and pituitary adenoma defines multiple endocrine neoplasia type 1, caused by loss of the menin tumor suppressor. The RET proto-oncogene produces the type 2 syndromes, which feature medullary thyroid carcinoma and pheochromocytoma rather than this combination.
  72. The correct answer is A (Fasting glucose of 131 mg/dL and hemoglobin A1c of 6.8 percent drawn from the same sample): Without unequivocal hyperglycemia, diagnosis needs two abnormal results, which can come from two different tests in the same sample. A fasting glucose of 126 mg/dL or higher and hemoglobin A1c of 6.5 percent or higher together satisfy this. A single test, a two-hour value under 200, prediabetic values, or a random glucose under 200 do not.
  73. The correct answer is C (Sinusoidal portal hypertension from cirrhosis, producing high gradient fluid with low protein content): The serum ascites albumin gradient is 2.4 minus 1.0, or 1.4 g/dL, which exceeds 1.1 and indicates portal hypertension. A total protein of 1.2 g/dL separates cirrhosis from cardiac ascites, where protein is usually above 2.5. The neutrophil count is below 250, so spontaneous bacterial peritonitis is not present.
  74. The correct answer is D (Incarcerated femoral hernia with small bowel obstruction through the narrow femoral ring): A firm, irreducible bulge below the inguinal ligament in an older woman is typical of a femoral hernia, which has a narrow, rigid ring and a high strangulation rate. Inguinal hernias present above the ligament, and obturator hernias seldom produce a palpable groin mass. Early surgical repair is needed.
  75. The correct answer is A (Defer cervical thrust techniques and use gentle muscle energy or counterstrain after vascular assessment): Dizziness and visual blurring with head rotation and extension suggest vertebrobasilar insufficiency, a contraindication to cervical thrust, so gentle indirect or muscle energy options are preferred after vascular assessment. Moving the neck through the provocative positions or thrusting in neutral does not remove the risk.
  76. The correct answer is A (Hyperinflated right lung with mediastinal shift away from the obstructed side on expiration): A witnessed choking episode followed by unilateral wheeze and decreased breath sounds suggests a bronchial foreign body. A ball-valve obstruction allows air in but not out, so the affected lung stays hyperinflated on expiratory films and the mediastinum shifts away from it. Complete obstruction would instead cause collapse.
  77. The correct answer is E (Disclose the error promptly, with expected effects and a monitoring plan, and file an incident report): Ethical practice and institutional policy call for prompt disclosure of an error that reached the patient, even when no harm has occurred, along with a plan to monitor and mitigate effects and an internal report. Concealment or waiting for harm to appear undermines trust. Notifying a licensing board first skips the system and the patient.
  78. The correct answer is C (Intraductal papilloma, a benign epithelial growth on a fibrovascular stalk within a lactiferous duct): Unilateral, single duct bloody discharge with a small intraductal mass and a normal mammogram points to an intraductal papilloma. Ductal carcinoma in situ can bleed, but it usually brings calcifications and rarely a discrete intraluminal mass at this age. Duct ectasia typically involves several ducts with thick, colored discharge.
  79. The correct answer is E (Perivascular glomeruloid structures around a central capillary, called Schiller-Duval bodies, lined by tumor cells): A markedly raised alpha-fetoprotein with a normal hCG identifies a yolk sac tumor, which forms Schiller-Duval bodies. Seminoma produces fried egg cells without alpha-fetoprotein, and choriocarcinoma makes hCG. Mixed or embryonal tumors usually show a more varied marker pattern.
  80. The correct answer is E (High-velocity low-amplitude thrust applied through the thoracic spine at the restrictive barrier): Steroid-induced osteopenia with an existing compression deformity makes a direct thrust at the restrictive barrier risky for fracture, so high-velocity low-amplitude treatment is the technique to avoid. Muscle energy, myofascial release, counterstrain, and functional methods all use low force and remain safe choices for the rib restriction.
  81. The correct answer is B (Increased, because hemoglobin from extravasated red cells in the alveoli binds the tracer gas): Blood within the alveolar spaces contains hemoglobin that takes up the inhaled carbon monoxide, so the measured uptake rises. This is a recognized artifact in alveolar hemorrhage syndromes such as anti-GBM disease. Most parenchymal and vascular lung diseases lower the value instead.
  82. The correct answer is E (Isotonic saline infusion to restore volume and increase urinary calcium excretion through reduced proximal reabsorption): Hypercalcemia causes nephrogenic diabetes insipidus and vomiting, so patients are volume depleted, and saline restores filtration and reduces proximal calcium reabsorption with sodium. Zoledronic acid should follow because it needs days to work, and loop diuretics are added only after euvolemia. Suppressed PTH with elevated PTHrP identifies humoral hypercalcemia of malignancy.
  83. The correct answer is E (Bicarbonate diuresis producing metabolic acidosis that offsets respiratory alkalosis and sustains hypoxic ventilation): Acetazolamide inhibits carbonic anhydrase in the proximal tubule, causing bicarbonate loss and a mild metabolic acidosis. This offsets the respiratory alkalosis that otherwise limits the hypoxic drive at high elevation, so ventilation and oxygenation improve. It does not block peripheral chemoreceptors, which are the sensors that drive the hyperventilation.
  84. The correct answer is A (Medial collateral ligament, anterior cruciate ligament and the medial meniscus tethered to it): A lateral blow to a planted knee produces valgus stress, tearing the medial collateral ligament and then the anterior cruciate ligament, and the medial meniscus fails because it is attached to the medial ligament. Anterior tibial translation confirms the anterior cruciate, whereas the lateral meniscus is not tethered to the lateral collateral.
  85. The correct answer is C (Double effect: the aim is pain relief, and earlier death is only a foreseen side effect): Double effect permits a treatment with a good intended effect even when a harmful effect is foreseen, if the harm is not the means to the good and the good outweighs it. Opioid titration for pain fits these conditions. Nonmaleficence is respected by proportionate titration, so it does not forbid the dose increase.
  86. The correct answer is B (Airborne precautions in a negative pressure room with an N95 respirator for staff): This presentation is classic measles, which spreads as small aerosolized particles that remain suspended for hours, so the child needs a negative pressure room and staff wearing N95 respirators. Droplet precautions suit organisms such as meningococcus or influenza, whose larger particles travel only short distances. Gown and gloves would not prevent inhalation of the virus.
  87. The correct answer is B (Somatic symptom disorder, with excessive thoughts and behaviors devoted to a persistent physical symptom): Somatic symptom disorder requires one or more distressing somatic symptoms plus disproportionate thoughts, anxiety or time devoted to them, regardless of a medical explanation. Illness anxiety disorder is the near miss, but it applies when symptoms are absent or mild. Factitious disorder and malingering involve conscious falsification, which the stem rules out.
  88. The correct answer is E (Rapid infusion of crystalloid and blood products to restore intravascular volume and ventricular preload): Low filling pressures with a low cardiac index and high resistance indicate hypovolemic shock from hemorrhage. Restoring preload with volume and blood increases stroke volume and cardiac output. Inotropes and vasopressors do not replace lost blood, and diuretics or nitrates would worsen the preload deficit.
  89. The correct answer is E (Right-on-right forward sacral torsion around a right oblique axis of the sacrum): A positive seated flexion test with a deep left sulcus and a posterior right inferior lateral angle fits a right-on-right forward torsion, and the negative spring test supports a forward rather than backward pattern. A unilateral flexed sacrum would put the posterior angle on the same side as the deep sulcus. A negative standing test argues against a primary innominate problem.
  90. The correct answer is B (Common plantar digital nerve, pinched beneath the deep transverse metatarsal ligament in the forefoot): Burning pain into adjacent toes with a Mulder click is Morton neuroma, a perineural fibrosis of the common plantar digital nerve beneath the deep transverse metatarsal ligament. Narrow shoes squeeze the forefoot, which explains the relief when they are removed.
  91. The correct answer is E (Misfolded CFTR protein degraded before reaching the apical membrane of epithelial cells): Chronic cough, pancreatic insufficiency with steatorrhea, failure to thrive and sweat chloride above 60 mEq/L establish cystic fibrosis. The most common mutation, delta F508, causes misfolding of the chloride channel so it is degraded and never reaches the apical membrane. Primary ciliary dyskinesia has a normal sweat test.
  92. The correct answer is E (Glucosylation of Rho GTPases by secreted toxins, disrupting the actin cytoskeleton and causing epithelial necrosis): Antibiotic exposure disrupts colonic flora, allowing Clostridioides difficile to release toxins A and B that glucosylate Rho GTPases, collapsing the cytoskeleton and producing the adherent yellow-white pseudomembranes seen at colonoscopy. Shiga toxin acts on ribosomes and typically causes bloody diarrhea after beef or produce exposure. Crohn colitis is granulomatous but is not tied to recent cephalosporin therapy.
  93. The correct answer is B (Visceral technique with diaphragm doming that draws the herniated stomach inferiorly through the hiatus): A sliding hiatal hernia is a displacement of the stomach through the esophageal hiatus, so a visceral technique with diaphragm doming targets the structure at fault. Rib raising and cervical work may help autonomic tone but do not address the herniation itself. Counterstrain and rib muscle energy treat musculoskeletal tender points, not the stomach position.
  94. The correct answer is E (Lower trunk of the brachial plexus together with the adjacent cervical sympathetic chain): Pain along the medial arm, ulnar hand wasting and Horner syndrome point to an apical lung tumor invading the lower trunk (C8, T1) and the cervical sympathetic chain. A peripheral ulnar or median entrapment cannot produce ptosis and miosis, and an upper trunk lesion would weaken the shoulder rather than the hand.
  95. The correct answer is A (Reduced renal conversion of 25-hydroxyvitamin D to calcitriol, impairing intestinal calcium absorption and mineralization): Failing nephrons lose 1-alpha hydroxylase activity, so calcitriol falls, gut calcium absorption drops and serum calcium decreases. Phosphate retention and low calcitriol then drive secondary hyperparathyroidism and renal osteodystrophy. A raised PTH shows the hypocalcemia is real, which argues against a simple albumin effect.
  96. The correct answer is C (Operate under emergency doctrine or court order, because parental religious refusal cannot override lifesaving care): When a child faces imminent death from a treatable condition, the state's interest in protecting the child overrides parental refusal, and physicians may treat under emergency doctrine or seek a court order. Respecting refusal fits a competent adult, not a young child. A minor's assent cannot substitute for lawful consent here.
  97. The correct answer is A (Irreversible inactivation of the hydrogen potassium ATPase on the canalicular membrane): Proton pump inhibitors are activated in the acidic canaliculus and bind covalently to the hydrogen potassium ATPase, blocking the final step of acid secretion. H2 blockers, antimuscarinics, and misoprostol act upstream and are less complete. Sucralfate protects ulcer bases and does not reduce acid output.
  98. The correct answer is D (Cyclosporine, which binds cyclophilin and inhibits calcineurin, reducing interleukin-2 transcription in T cells): Gingival hyperplasia, hirsutism and hypertension are characteristic of cyclosporine, which complexes with cyclophilin to inhibit calcineurin. Tacrolimus shares the calcineurin mechanism but binds FKBP-12 and more often causes tremor, hyperglycemia and alopecia. Sirolimus acts on mTOR, and mycophenolate and azathioprine act on nucleotide synthesis.
  99. The correct answer is D (Anterior spinal artery branches from the left vertebral artery feeding the medial medulla): Medial medullary syndrome involves the pyramid (contralateral hemiparesis sparing the face), the medial lemniscus (contralateral loss of vibration and position sense) and the hypoglossal fibers (tongue deviation toward the side of the lesion). The left medulla is infarcted, supplied by anterior spinal artery branches. Spinothalamic fibers lie laterally, which explains the normal pain and temperature sensation.
  100. The correct answer is B (Multiphase contrast enhanced pancreatic protocol CT to define the mass and vascular involvement): Painless jaundice with a palpable nontender gallbladder (Courvoisier sign), weight loss and new diabetes strongly suggest pancreatic head adenocarcinoma. Pancreas protocol CT defines the mass and vascular involvement, which determines resectability. CA 19-9 supports monitoring but cannot establish the diagnosis.

Practice Test 9 Answer Key

  1. The correct answer is E (Right oculomotor fascicles and the adjacent right cerebral peduncle corticospinal fibers): In Weber syndrome a midbrain infarct involves the exiting oculomotor fascicles, causing ipsilateral ptosis, a dilated pupil and a down and out eye, and the cerebral peduncle, whose corticospinal fibers have not yet crossed, causing contralateral hemiparesis. Corticobulbar fibers explain the contralateral lower face droop. A pontine abducens lesion would instead cause failure of abduction.
  2. The correct answer is E (Cortisol draining from the adrenal cortex induces phenylethanolamine N-methyltransferase, which methylates norepinephrine to epinephrine): Blood draining from the adrenal cortex bathes the medulla in high cortisol, which induces the enzyme that converts norepinephrine to epinephrine. Extra-adrenal paragangliomas lack this exposure and therefore secrete mainly norepinephrine, producing a normetanephrine predominant pattern. Catechol-O-methyltransferase forms the metanephrine metabolites afterward and does not make epinephrine.
  3. The correct answer is D (Supportive care with nasal suctioning and fluids, adding oxygen if saturation falls below 90 percent): Bronchiolitis in an infant, most often from respiratory syncytial virus, is managed supportively with hydration, nasal clearing and oxygen only when saturation is persistently low. Bronchodilators, steroids, antibiotics, routine hypertonic saline and chest physiotherapy have not shown consistent benefit.
  4. The correct answer is B (Respiratory circulatory model, where thoracic and diaphragm mechanics drive fluid return and tissue nutrition): Treating the thoracic inlet and the breathing muscle to improve venous and lymphatic return is the respiratory circulatory model. The biomechanical model also involves structure, but its goal is posture and load transfer and not fluid movement.
  5. The correct answer is D (ALA synthase, which condenses glycine and succinyl-CoA in mitochondria and requires pyridoxal phosphate): Aminolevulinate synthase is the rate-limiting, heme-inhibited first step, condensing glycine and succinyl-CoA in mitochondria with pyridoxal phosphate as cofactor. Barbiturates and fasting induce it, which is why they precipitate attacks in porphobilinogen deaminase deficiency. The later enzymes are not subject to this regulation.
  6. The correct answer is C (Xanthine oxidase inhibition blocking breakdown of a thiopurine metabolite, causing toxic accumulation in marrow): Azathioprine is converted to 6-mercaptopurine, which xanthine oxidase inactivates. Allopurinol blocks that enzyme, so the active thiopurine metabolites accumulate and cause marrow suppression, and the dose of azathioprine must be reduced by about two thirds. The other mechanisms do not describe this interaction.
  7. The correct answer is C (Increased acetylcholine persistence in the synaptic cleft allowing more binding to remaining receptors): Edrophonium is a short-acting acetylcholinesterase inhibitor. In myasthenia gravis, autoantibodies reduce functional nicotinic receptors, so blocking acetylcholine breakdown lets the transmitter remain in the cleft longer and bind the remaining receptors, briefly restoring end plate potentials. It does not stimulate receptors directly or remove antibodies.
  8. The correct answer is D (Altered tension at the jugular foramen affecting vagus nerve outflow to bronchial smooth muscle and glands): The vagus nerve passes through the jugular foramen, which lies between the occiput and the temporal bone near the occipitomastoid suture, so restriction there can alter its outflow to the bronchi. The thoracic sympathetic supply is a different pathway, and the pelvic splanchnic, facial, and phrenic nerves do not carry the vagal parasympathetic supply to the lungs.
  9. The correct answer is E (Inhibition of thymidylate synthase, depleting thymidine nucleotides needed for DNA synthesis): 5-Fluorouracil is a pyrimidine analog whose metabolite forms a stable complex with thymidylate synthase, blocking thymidine production and DNA synthesis in rapidly dividing cells. Methotrexate targets dihydrofolate reductase, taxanes act on microtubules, anthracyclines intercalate DNA, and alkylators cross-link guanine.
  10. The correct answer is B (Anion gap metabolic acidosis plus separate primary respiratory alkalosis, with carbon dioxide under expected compensation): The anion gap is 140 minus 104 minus 14, or 22 mEq/L, so a gap metabolic acidosis is present. The Winter formula predicts a carbon dioxide near 29 mm Hg, but the measured value is 20, so a second primary respiratory alkalosis from direct respiratory center stimulation coexists, the classic salicylate pattern. Simple compensation would leave the pH acidemic.
  11. The correct answer is B (About 5 liters, from total body water times the fractional excess of sodium above 140): Free water deficit equals total body water multiplied by the quantity of measured sodium over 140, minus one. With 35 liters of total body water, 35 times 160 over 140 minus 35 gives about 5 liters. A factor of 0.6 would give 6 liters, which ignores the stated lower water fraction in older men. Correction should be slow, lowering sodium by no more than about 10 mEq/L per day.
  12. The correct answer is C (Arrange emergency MRI and surgical consultation for suspected cauda equina compression before any spinal treatment): Urinary retention, saddle anesthesia, and bilateral leg weakness are red flags for cauda equina syndrome, which needs emergency imaging and surgical evaluation. Any manipulative treatment, even gentle, delays definitive care, and the somatic dysfunction at L4 does not explain the deficits.
  13. The correct answer is B (Hysterosalpingography to assess tubal patency and the uterine cavity contour after her prior pelvic infection): Regular cycles with premenstrual symptoms suggest ovulation, and the semen analysis is normal, so prior chlamydial infection makes tubal scarring the leading cause. Hysterosalpingography is the standard first test of tubal patency and the cavity. Laparoscopy is reserved for cases where imaging suggests pathology or other evaluation fails.
  14. The correct answer is C (Anterior cruciate ligament, the primary restraint against the shin sliding ahead of the thigh): A noncontact pivoting injury with an audible pop, rapid hemarthrosis, and increased anterior tibial translation on Lachman testing indicates an anterior cruciate ligament tear. The posterior cruciate ligament resists posterior translation. Collateral ligament and meniscal injuries produce different instability and examination findings.
  15. The correct answer is D (Activation of the stimulatory G protein by ADP-ribosylation, raising cyclic AMP in enterocytes): The A subunit of cholera toxin ADP-ribosylates the alpha subunit of Gs, locking it active so adenylate cyclase drives cyclic AMP and chloride and water secretion. Inhibiting the inhibitory protein is the pertussis pattern and does not apply here. Heat-stable E. coli toxin acts through cyclic GMP and rarely produces output of this volume.
  16. The correct answer is C (Slow acetylator status prolonging drug exposure and depleting pyridoxine, preventable with vitamin B6 supplementation): Isoniazid is inactivated by N-acetyltransferase, and slow acetylators accumulate the drug, which binds pyridoxine and increases its urinary loss, causing peripheral neuropathy. Vitamin B6 prevents this. Rapid acetylators are more prone to a different concern and do not develop this neuropathy more readily.
  17. The correct answer is D (Delayed type hypersensitivity with T cell infiltration of the interstitium after drug exposure): Fever, rash, eosinophiluria and a rising creatinine one to two weeks after a beta-lactam define acute interstitial nephritis, a T cell mediated delayed hypersensitivity reaction. Stopping the drug is the key treatment. Acute tubular necrosis would show muddy brown casts and no allergic signs.
  18. The correct answer is B (High-velocity low-amplitude thrust, a direct technique that risks fracture in osteoporotic vertebrae and ribs): Glucocorticoid-induced osteoporosis with a prior vertebral fracture makes a thrust technique hazardous because direct force at the restrictive barrier can fracture weakened vertebrae or ribs. Counterstrain, functional technique and facilitated positional release apply little force, and low-intensity muscle energy is also acceptable. The somatic dysfunction can still be treated safely.
  19. The correct answer is A (Substitute insulin glargine, a basal analog with flat absorption, for the evening intermediate acting insulin): NPH peaks roughly 4 to 10 hours after injection, so a bedtime dose peaks in the early morning and causes the nadir near 3 AM, followed by a rebound rise from counterregulatory hormones. A peakless basal analog such as glargine removes the peak. Raising NPH or adding rapid insulin at bedtime would deepen the overnight low.
  20. The correct answer is E (Sjogren syndrome, an autoimmune destruction of salivary and lacrimal glands that also raises lymphoma risk): Dry mouth, dry eyes, bilateral parotid enlargement, anti-Ro positivity, and periductal lymphocytic infiltrates on lip biopsy establish Sjogren syndrome. Sarcoidosis can enlarge the parotids but typically shows hilar adenopathy, uveitis, or facial palsy, all absent here. Mumps would be acute and painful rather than chronic.
  21. The correct answer is C (Klebsiella pneumoniae, an encapsulated gram-negative rod producing thick mucoid exudate in debilitated hosts): Chronic alcohol use predisposes to aspiration and to pneumonia from Klebsiella, whose thick capsule produces heavy mucoid, blood-tinged sputum and a lobe so full of exudate that the fissure bulges. Streptococcus pneumoniae is the tempting common cause, but it does not typically give this sticky currant jelly sputum or fissure bulging, and Klebsiella tends to cause abscesses and necrosis.
  22. The correct answer is E (Varenicline, a partial nicotinic receptor agonist that eases craving and blunts the reward from smoking): Bupropion lowers the seizure threshold and is contraindicated in seizure disorders and in bulimia, which removes it despite its efficacy. Varenicline is among the most effective single agents, carries no seizure-threshold concern here, and suits a patient whose patch monotherapy failed. Nortriptyline also lowers the threshold and is a later line.
  23. The correct answer is D (Negative pregnancy testing and iPLEDGE enrollment with counseling on using two forms of contraception): Isotretinoin is a potent teratogen, so US prescribing requires registration in the iPLEDGE program, negative pregnancy testing, and counseling on two forms of contraception. Cardiac and bone imaging and biopsy are not baseline requirements, and a longer antibiotic trial is not mandated when severe nodular disease has already prompted referral.
  24. The correct answer is E (Weak pelvic floor support lets the bladder neck descend, so rising abdominal pressure exceeds closure pressure): Childbirth injury weakens pelvic floor and urethral support, so effort raises bladder pressure above the pressure that keeps the outlet closed, producing stress incontinence. A small residual volume argues against overflow, and the lack of urgency argues against detrusor overactivity. A fistula leaks continuously.
  25. The correct answer is A (Norepinephrine, which constricts arterioles through alpha-1 receptors with modest cardiac stimulation): Norepinephrine is the recommended first-line pressor in septic shock because alpha-1 mediated vasoconstriction restores mean arterial pressure while beta-1 effects maintain output. Dobutamine and isoproterenol can lower resistance and worsen hypotension. Vasopressin is typically added to norepinephrine rather than used first.
  26. The correct answer is A (Villous blunting with crypt hyperplasia and increased intraepithelial lymphocytes in the duodenal mucosa): Elevated anti tissue transglutaminase antibody with dermatitis herpetiformis and refractory iron deficiency indicates celiac disease, in which gluten driven T cell injury blunts the villi and expands the crypts. Giardia can also flatten villi but would be identified by trophozoites and would not explain the antibody or the rash.
  27. The correct answer is E (Ventilation-perfusion scintigraphy, which avoids iodinated contrast and works best when lung fields are clear): With a normal chest radiograph, a ventilation-perfusion scan is interpretable, and it spares a patient with advanced kidney disease an iodinated contrast load. D-dimer is only used to exclude embolism when pretest probability is low or intermediate, so it is not appropriate here. Compression ultrasound can support treatment if positive, but a negative study does not exclude embolism.
  28. The correct answer is A (Carbamazepine, which enhances renal collecting duct responsiveness to antidiuretic hormone): Carbamazepine is first-line for trigeminal neuralgia and is a well-known cause of the syndrome of inappropriate antidiuretic hormone secretion. The patient is euvolemic with low serum osmolality and concentrated urine. Valproate causes hyperammonemia, and topiramate causes metabolic acidosis rather than hyponatremia.
  29. The correct answer is E (Failed Na-K ATPase pump function from ATP depletion, letting sodium and water accumulate): Early ischemic injury lowers ATP, so the Na-K ATPase fails and sodium and water enter the cell, producing swelling and clear vacuoles that are still reversible. Calcium influx with membrane degradation belongs to irreversible injury, and caspase activation is apoptosis, not hypoxic swelling.
  30. The correct answer is C (Serratus anterior, supplied by a nerve running superficially along the lateral thoracic wall): Pushing against resistance makes serratus anterior hold the scapula against the thorax, and its long thoracic nerve is vulnerable on the lateral chest wall during axillary dissection. Latissimus dorsi also lies in the operative field, but its loss weakens adduction and internal rotation rather than producing scapular winging.
  31. The correct answer is A (Intravenous thrombolytic therapy to restore flow through the occluded intracranial vessel within the treatment window): She is within the window, has a disabling deficit, normal glucose, no anticoagulant, and CT excluding hemorrhage, so thrombolysis is indicated. Her pressure of 172/94 is below the threshold that must be lowered before treatment, and permissive hypertension is otherwise tolerated. Heparin and aspirin alone do not substitute for reperfusion, and craniectomy is reserved for later malignant edema.
  32. The correct answer is B (APC tumor suppressor gene on chromosome 5q, which regulates beta catenin degradation): Familial adenomatous polyposis is autosomal dominant from mutation of the APC gene on chromosome 5q, which allows beta catenin accumulation and produces hundreds of adenomas with near certain cancer without colectomy. Lynch syndrome produces few polyps, and hamartomatous syndromes involve STK11 or SMAD4.
  33. The correct answer is E (Continuous receptor occupation downregulates pituitary gonadotrophs, lowering luteinizing and follicle stimulating hormone secretion): Pulsatile GnRH stimulates gonadotrophs, but continuous agonist exposure first causes a flare and then receptor downregulation and desensitization. Luteinizing and follicle stimulating hormone fall, ovarian estradiol production drops, and menopausal symptoms follow. Antagonists block the receptor from the start with no flare, which distinguishes them from the agonist mechanism here.
  34. The correct answer is E (Medial meniscus, with a displaced bucket-handle fragment wedged within the femorotibial compartment): A twisting injury with joint line tenderness, a McMurray click and true mechanical locking indicates a meniscal tear, and the medial side is shown by the tenderness. A negative Lachman test argues against anterior cruciate rupture, and a collateral sprain would give laxity to valgus stress rather than locking.
  35. The correct answer is B (Relative risk of 2.0, indicating twice the cumulative incidence among nurses with the exposure): Cumulative incidence is 5 percent in night-shift nurses (50 of 1,000) and 2.5 percent in day-shift nurses (100 of 4,000), so relative risk is 5 divided by 2.5, or 2.0. The absolute excess is 2.5 percentage points, which is a separate measure and not the relative risk. Cohort designs yield risks directly, so reporting an odds ratio is unnecessary.
  36. The correct answer is A (Reduced cardiac output through beta-1 blockade plus splanchnic vasoconstriction from unopposed alpha activity after beta-2 blockade): Nonselective beta blockers lower portal inflow by reducing cardiac output through beta-1 blockade and by blocking splanchnic beta-2 mediated vasodilation, leaving alpha tone to constrict mesenteric vessels. Terlipressin also constricts splanchnic vessels but through vasopressin receptors, so it is not the mechanism of nadolol.
  37. The correct answer is B (Delirium due to acute infection superimposed on mild underlying cognitive impairment in an older resident): Acute onset over days, inattention, fluctuating arousal, and an infectious trigger define delirium, which is common in older patients with baseline cognitive impairment. Alzheimer disease progresses over years and preserves attention until late. Treatment targets the infection and precipitating factors.
  38. The correct answer is B (Intravenous N-acetylcysteine, replenishing glutathione stores to neutralize the reactive toxic metabolite of CYP oxidation): The nomogram and normal acid-base status indicate acetaminophen, whose toxic metabolite NAPQI overwhelms glutathione and injures the liver. N-acetylcysteine restores glutathione and works best when started early, even before enzymes rise. Charcoal helps only within a short window after ingestion and does not replace the antidote.
  39. The correct answer is E (Perform screening and stabilizing care, including delivery, before any transfer, with payment status playing no role): EMTALA requires a hospital emergency department to screen anyone who presents and to stabilize an emergency condition, including active labor, before transfer, whatever the person's ability to pay or immigration status. Payment checks or citizenship checks cannot delay screening. A transfer before stabilization is allowed only under narrow conditions that do not include inability to bill.
  40. The correct answer is C (Cervical or vaginal laceration causing ongoing venous or arterial bleeding from the birth canal): Bleeding that continues with a firm, contracted uterus and an intact placenta points to trauma of the cervix or vaginal wall, especially after a rapid delivery. Atony causes a soft, boggy fundus, and inversion removes the palpable fundus. Careful inspection and repair of the birth canal controls this bleeding.
  41. The correct answer is A (Albumin binding of unconjugated bilirubin prevents glomerular filtration, so none reaches the urine): In hemolysis the excess is unconjugated bilirubin, which is lipid soluble and tightly albumin bound, so it is not filtered and the urine stays acholuric. Increased urobilinogen reflects the larger conjugated load handled by gut bacteria and enterohepatic recycling. Conjugated bilirubin, being water soluble, would appear in urine in cholestasis.
  42. The correct answer is A (Osimertinib, a third generation inhibitor of the mutant epidermal growth factor receptor tyrosine kinase): An exon 19 deletion in the epidermal growth factor receptor gene is a sensitizing mutation, and a tyrosine kinase inhibitor such as osimertinib is preferred first line, with good central nervous system penetration. Crizotinib is tempting because ALK rearrangement also occurs in never smokers, but this tumor carries a different driver. Immunotherapy has little benefit in tumors with this mutation.
  43. The correct answer is C (Setting, creating a calm environment with seating and few interruptions before the discussion starts): The first step of the SPIKES protocol is Setting up the interview, which includes privacy, sitting down, involving relevant others and managing interruptions such as a pager. The Perception and Invitation steps occur after this preparation inside the conversation. Knowledge and Empathy follow later still.
  44. The correct answer is E (Sulfapyridine moiety impairing folate absorption, with reversible reduction in sperm number): Sulfasalazine is split into mesalamine and sulfapyridine, and the sulfapyridine part impairs folate absorption and reversibly lowers sperm count. This yields macrocytic anemia that responds to folate. Mesalamine nephritis would not cause macrocytosis, and the other options describe drugs the patient is not receiving.
  45. The correct answer is E (Increased afterload from a stenotic outflow valve, raising peak pressure and reducing stroke volume): In aortic stenosis the ventricle must generate a higher peak pressure to open a stenotic valve, so the loop is tall; ejection is limited, so stroke volume falls and end-systolic volume rises, making the loop narrower. Regurgitation would instead widen the loop and raise end-diastolic volume, the opposite of the tall, narrowed loop shown.
  46. The correct answer is B (Cholecystokinin, causing gallbladder contraction, sphincter of Oddi relaxation and pancreatic enzyme secretion): Cholecystokinin is released by I cells in response to fat and protein, contracts the gallbladder, relaxes the sphincter of Oddi and stimulates acinar enzyme secretion. Secretin is tempting since it is also a duodenal hormone, but it is released by acid and drives bicarbonate secretion.
  47. The correct answer is D (Risk follows asbestos exposure after decades of latency and is not increased by cigarette smoking): Malignant mesothelioma is tied to asbestos exposure with a latency of 20 to 40 years, and unlike bronchogenic carcinoma, smoking does not add to the risk. Calretinin positivity supports mesothelial origin. Silica and beryllium cause other pneumoconioses but are not recognized causes of this tumor.
  48. The correct answer is A (Agonism of presynaptic GABA B receptors reducing excitatory neurotransmitter release in the dorsal horn): Baclofen is a GABA B agonist that hyperpolarizes presynaptic terminals and reduces excitatory transmission in the spinal cord, treating spasticity. Abrupt cessation, particularly of intrathecal therapy, can cause seizures, hallucinations, and rebound spasticity. Dantrolene, tizanidine, and benzodiazepines act through different targets.
  49. The correct answer is E (Demyelination raising capacitance and lowering resistance so current dissipates before reaching the next node): Myelin raises membrane resistance and lowers capacitance, letting current flow along the axon to the next node of Ranvier with minimal loss. Demyelination in multiple sclerosis reverses both properties, so more current leaks and the length constant shortens, delaying or blocking saltatory conduction. A larger diameter lowers internal resistance and would speed conduction, not slow it.
  50. The correct answer is C (Unabsorbed lactose drawing water into the lumen and fermenting into gas and organic acids): Lactase deficiency leaves lactose in the lumen, where it is osmotically active and fermented by colonic bacteria to gas and acids, lowering stool pH and creating an osmotic gap that resolves with fasting. Secretory diarrhea has a normal gap and continues during fasting, and celiac disease would show villous blunting.
  51. The correct answer is A (Serial manipulation and casting by the Ponseti method, usually followed by percutaneous Achilles tenotomy): Congenital talipes equinovarus is treated first with weekly serial manipulation and casting by the Ponseti method, often with percutaneous Achilles tenotomy, then abduction bracing to prevent relapse. Observation is appropriate only for positional deformities that correct easily. Surgery and fusion are reserved for resistant or neglected cases.
  52. The correct answer is D (Bacillus anthracis producing edema factor and lethal factor with an antiphagocytic poly-D-glutamate capsule): Inhalational anthrax produces biphasic illness with hemorrhagic mediastinitis and large encapsulated gram-positive rods on culture. Edema factor is an adenylate cyclase and lethal factor is a protease, both delivered by protective antigen. Yersinia pestis can also cause a bioterrorism pneumonia, but it is a gram-negative bipolar staining rod and does not match the culture finding.
  53. The correct answer is D (Acidity raises the ionized fraction of the weak base, limiting penetration into the axon): Lidocaine is a weak base, and in acidic inflamed tissue more of it is protonated, but only the uncharged form crosses the nerve membrane to reach the sodium channel from inside. Amides are cleared by the liver rather than tissue esterases, and increased perfusion plays a smaller role than the pH effect.
  54. The correct answer is B (Inspiratory rise in right ventricular filling bows the septum leftward and lowers left ventricular stroke volume): With a fixed pericardial volume, inspiration increases right ventricular filling and the septum shifts toward the left ventricle. Left ventricular filling and stroke volume fall, producing pulsus paradoxus greater than 10 mm Hg. Pulmonary vein pooling explains only a small normal fall, and venous return actually increases with inspiration.
  55. The correct answer is C (Hemidesmosomal proteins BP180 and BP230 anchoring basal keratinocytes to the basement membrane): Bullous pemphigoid features tense subepidermal bullae in older adults, a negative Nikolsky sign and linear basement membrane IgG and C3 caused by antibodies to BP180 and BP230. Desmogleins are the targets in pemphigus, which gives flaccid intraepidermal blisters with oral erosions.
  56. The correct answer is E (Exhalation dysfunction of ribs 5 to 7, treating rib 5 as the key rib): Ribs that fail to rise on inhalation are stuck in exhalation, so this is an exhalation dysfunction. In exhalation dysfunction the uppermost rib of the group is the key rib because it restricts upward motion of the ribs below it, whereas in inhalation dysfunction the lowest rib is key. Treating the lowest or middle rib applies the wrong rule.
  57. The correct answer is B (45,X with streak gonads from accelerated oocyte loss, short stature and aortic coarctation): Short stature, a webbed or low hairline, a broad chest, hypertension with delayed femoral pulses from coarctation, and hypergonadotropic primary amenorrhea indicate Turner syndrome, 45,X. Gonadal dysgenesis alone would not cause short stature or coarctation, and mosaic forms often show some puberty. Echocardiography and renal imaging follow the diagnosis.
  58. The correct answer is A (Start intravenous acyclovir immediately while polymerase chain reaction testing of cerebrospinal fluid is pending): Fever, behavioral change, olfactory hallucination, seizure, temporal lobe MRI changes, and lymphocytic fluid with red cells indicate herpes simplex encephalitis. Mortality is high without early therapy, so acyclovir is started empirically and not delayed for polymerase chain reaction results. Antibacterial therapy alone or steroids alone do not treat the virus.
  59. The correct answer is C (Recall bias, because parents of a sick child scrutinize their past exposures more intensely): True exposure was similar, yet the mothers of affected infants reported more of it, which is the signature of differential recall driven by the search for a cause. Blinded interviewers and a structured instrument make interviewer bias unlikely. Non-differential misclassification would push results toward the null rather than create a spurious excess.
  60. The correct answer is C (Creatine kinase MB, which returns to baseline within about three days after one infarct): CK-MB rises within hours and normalizes in 48 to 72 hours, so a new rise after several days points to reinfarction. Troponin stays elevated for up to two weeks after a single event and so cannot separate old injury from new. Aspartate aminotransferase and LDH are neither cardiac specific nor timed usefully.
  61. The correct answer is D (Supraspinatus tendon, compressed beneath the acromion during early elevation of the arm): The empty can position isolates the supraspinatus, whose tendon passes under the acromion and is the most commonly torn cuff tendon in older manual workers. Infraspinatus and teres minor would instead weaken external rotation with the elbow at the side, which is not the maneuver reproducing the pain here.
  62. The correct answer is D (Chronic hypoxic pulmonary vasoconstriction with arteriolar remodeling that increases pulmonary vascular resistance): Right heart failure from chronic lung disease is cor pulmonale, and the driver is alveolar hypoxia, which constricts pulmonary arterioles and eventually remodels them. Normal left heart function and valves rule out postcapillary causes. Thromboembolic obstruction and heritable arterial disease are possible causes of right heart strain but are not suggested here.
  63. The correct answer is E (Non-ST-elevation myocardial infarction, since the rising troponin confirms myocyte necrosis in the subendocardium): ST depression with chest pain and rising troponin indicates infarction limited to the subendocardium, which is a non-ST-elevation MI. Unstable angina has the same ECG pattern but no troponin rise, which is the detail the panel supplies. ST elevation would signal transmural injury.
  64. The correct answer is C (Common variable immunodeficiency, with impaired B cell differentiation into plasma cells and low serum antibodies): Low IgG, IgA and IgM with normal B cell numbers and onset of infections and giardiasis in young adulthood indicate common variable immunodeficiency, where B cells fail to become plasma cells. Agammaglobulinemia would show absent B cells and presents in infancy in males, while isolated IgA deficiency keeps normal IgG.
  65. The correct answer is A (Dementia with Lewy bodies, in which dopamine-blocking antipsychotic drugs cause severe sensitivity reactions): Fluctuating cognition, well-formed visual hallucinations, and parkinsonism with cognitive decline beginning before or within a year of motor signs define dementia with Lewy bodies. Severe reactions to neuroleptics are characteristic. In Parkinson disease dementia, motor symptoms precede dementia by more than a year.
  66. The correct answer is E (Antibodies against cyclic citrullinated peptide, formed against proteins modified by peptidylarginine deiminase): Anti-CCP antibodies recognize citrullinated proteins and are highly specific for rheumatoid arthritis, and they may precede symptoms. Rheumatoid factor is frequently positive in other chronic inflammatory conditions and infections, which lowers its specificity. Anti-Smith and anti-topoisomerase antibodies mark lupus and systemic sclerosis.
  67. The correct answer is B (5.0 percent, from new cases divided by the disease-free population at baseline): Only the 8,000 residents without diabetes on January 1 were at risk, so cumulative incidence is 400 divided by 8,000, or 5.0 percent. Including the 2,000 prevalent cases in the denominator understates the risk, and the combined 2,400 cases over 10,000 is end-of-year prevalence rather than incidence.
  68. The correct answer is D (Recruitment of collapsed alveoli, increasing functional residual capacity and reducing intrapulmonary shunt fraction): End-expiratory pressure keeps injured, fluid-filled alveoli open, which raises functional residual capacity and converts shunted blood into ventilated, oxygenated blood. PEEP typically lowers cardiac output rather than raising it, and it does not change hemoglobin affinity or anatomic dead space in any useful way.
  69. The correct answer is E (Salmonella species, favored by intestinal translocation and impaired reticuloendothelial clearance in sickle cell disease): Patients with sickle cell disease have repeated bowel and bone infarcts that allow enteric bacteria to seed bone, and they have poor clearance of organisms. Salmonella is the classic cause of osteomyelitis in this group, although Staphylococcus aureus remains common overall. Encapsulated organisms cause sepsis rather than typical bone infection.
  70. The correct answer is C (Deferasirox, a tridentate chelator that binds ferric iron and promotes fecal excretion): Chronic transfusion causes iron overload that the body cannot excrete, and chelation is needed. Deferasirox is an oral, once daily iron chelator that excretes iron in stool, which fits a patient unable to use subcutaneous pump infusions. Succimer, dimercaprol and edetate target lead or other heavy metals rather than iron.
  71. The correct answer is D (Thymoma, an epithelial neoplasm of the thymus linked to antibodies against postsynaptic receptors): Fatigable ptosis and diplopia suggest myasthenia gravis, and about a third of these patients have a thymoma in the anterior mediastinum. Teratoma and goiter also arise anteriorly but are not associated with the syndrome, and neurogenic tumors are posterior. The new running program only explains why symptoms appeared late in the day.
  72. The correct answer is A (Increased pulmonary capillary hydrostatic pressure from left ventricular failure with an intact pleural surface): The pleural to serum protein ratio is about 0.33 and the pleural lactate dehydrogenase is far below two thirds of the upper limit of normal, so the fluid meets neither of Light criteria and is a transudate. With orthopnea and a high natriuretic peptide, left heart failure raising capillary hydrostatic pressure is the cause. Inflammation and lymphatic blockage would give an exudate.
  73. The correct answer is D (Infarct of the left subthalamic nucleus removing excitatory drive to the globus pallidus internus): Hemiballismus follows a lesion of the contralateral subthalamic nucleus, most often a lacunar infarct in a hypertensive or embolic patient. Loss of excitatory glutamate drive to the globus pallidus internus reduces its inhibition of the thalamus, so cortical motor output is excessive. The left lesion explains right-sided movements. Chorea from caudate loss is slower and develops gradually.
  74. The correct answer is A (Elevated NADH to NAD+ ratio diverting pyruvate to lactate and oxaloacetate to malate, inhibiting gluconeogenesis): Ethanol oxidation generates large amounts of NADH, which drives pyruvate to lactate and oxaloacetate to malate and so removes the substrates for gluconeogenesis. After glycogen runs out in fasting, glucose falls. Glycogenolysis is not the issue because stores are already depleted.
  75. The correct answer is C (Iris bowing forward against the trabecular meshwork, obstructing aqueous drainage at a narrow angle): A hyperopic eye has a shallow anterior chamber, and dim light plus an anticholinergic antihistamine dilates the pupil so the peripheral iris blocks the angle, causing acute angle-closure glaucoma. Chronic open-angle glaucoma is painless and gradual, so it does not produce an acute red eye with a fixed mid-dilated pupil.
  76. The correct answer is B (Increasing the frequency of chloride channel opening at the GABA-A receptor, enhancing neuronal hyperpolarization): Benzodiazepines bind an allosteric site on the GABA-A receptor and increase how often the chloride channel opens when GABA is bound, hyperpolarizing neurons and reducing anxiety within minutes to hours. Barbiturates are the tempting alternative because they also act at this receptor, but they lengthen channel opening and can open it without GABA, which explains their narrower safety margin.
  77. The correct answer is B (Extracellular beta-amyloid plaques with intraneuronal neurofibrillary tangles of hyperphosphorylated tau): Slowly progressive amnestic dementia with hippocampal atrophy is typical of Alzheimer disease. Neuropathology shows extracellular amyloid plaques and intraneuronal tau tangles. Lewy bodies, Pick bodies, spongiform change, and lacunar infarcts belong to other dementias with different clinical profiles.
  78. The correct answer is A (Hydrochlorothiazide, which increases distal tubule calcium reabsorption and lowers urinary calcium excretion): Thiazides inhibit the sodium chloride cotransporter in the distal tubule, which increases calcium reabsorption and reduces urinary calcium, lowering stone formation and aiding bone density. Loop diuretics increase calcium loss and worsen stones and osteopenia. The other agents do not lower urinary calcium meaningfully.
  79. The correct answer is E (Admit for inpatient psychiatric stabilization with continuous safety observation until risk is reassessed): A specific plan with the means already gathered marks high imminent risk, so she needs a protected setting with continuous observation and a full psychiatric evaluation. Calm behavior does not lower risk, and a safety contract or outpatient follow-up does not provide enough protection.
  80. The correct answer is E (Loss of skeletal muscle contraction removes the external squeeze that propels lymph past one-way valves): Lymph moves largely through the squeezing action of working skeletal muscle on vessels that contain one-way valves, so three weeks of bed rest leaves fluid pooled in the feet. The normal albumin, creatinine, and echocardiogram argue against low oncotic pressure, renal retention, and cardiac causes, which is why a pedal pump is reasonable.
  81. The correct answer is B (Recombinant urate oxidase converts uric acid to the more soluble allantoin for renal excretion): Rasburicase is recombinant urate oxidase, an enzyme humans lack, and it converts uric acid to allantoin, which is far more soluble. It acts quickly on existing urate in tumor lysis syndrome. Allopurinol only prevents new formation, uricosurics are unsafe with heavy urate loads, and interleukin-1 blockade treats inflammation rather than urate.
  82. The correct answer is E (Biceps femoris tethering the head backward, with irritation of the superficial peroneal nerve): The biceps femoris inserts on the fibular head, and a tight or strained muscle pulls it posteriorly and can irritate the superficial peroneal branch crossing the lateral leg. Intact dorsiflexion argues against deep peroneal involvement. Peroneus longus, semimembranosus and tibialis anterior do not fit the posterior position or the dorsal foot numbness.
  83. The correct answer is D (Superficial partial-thickness injury sparing deep dermal structures and healing without scarring): Blisters, a moist pink blanching base, brisk capillary refill and severe pain indicate that dermal appendages and papillary dermis are intact, so this is a superficial partial-thickness (superficial second-degree) burn. Deep partial-thickness burns are drier, mottled, less painful and slower to heal, and an epidermal-only burn would not blister.
  84. The correct answer is A (Complete Achilles tendon rupture, with weak residual motion from the deep posterior compartment flexors): A sudden snap, a palpable gap, and an absent plantar flexion response on the Thompson test indicate complete Achilles rupture, and fluoroquinolone exposure increases that risk. Residual weak plantar flexion comes from the flexor hallucis longus and tibialis posterior, which should not mislead the examiner. Tendinopathy and bursitis do not cause a gap.
  85. The correct answer is D (Iron deficiency with depleted storage iron and raised transferrin availability in the circulation): Low ferritin, low iron, high total iron-binding capacity, and low transferrin saturation with a microcytic anemia and wide red cell distribution width indicate iron deficiency. The Mentzer index above 13 also favors iron deficiency over thalassemia trait. Chronic disease raises ferritin and lowers binding capacity.
  86. The correct answer is A (Amplification of a transcription factor gene that drives unchecked proliferation of neural crest derived cells): Elevated urinary catecholamine metabolites with a midline crossing mass and small round blue cell rosettes indicate neuroblastoma, and amplification of the N-myc transcription factor gene marks the aggressive form. Wilms tumor usually stays unilateral and does not raise catecholamine metabolites, and retinoblastoma arises in the retina.
  87. The correct answer is A (T5 to T9 paraspinal region, matching the sympathetic outflow to the stomach and upper gut): Gastric disease facilitates sympathetic segments from T5 to T9, so tissue texture changes appear in those paraspinal levels. Lower levels correspond to the kidney, colon and pelvic organs, and sacral findings relate to parasympathetic supply rather than a paraspinal sympathetic reflex.
  88. The correct answer is B (Loss of angiotensin II constriction of efferent arterioles reducing filtration pressure in stenotic kidneys): When renal perfusion is reduced by artery stenosis, efferent constriction from angiotensin II maintains filtration pressure. An ACE inhibitor removes that constriction, so filtration falls and creatinine and potassium rise, with a bland urinalysis. The prostaglandin mechanism describes nonsteroidal drugs, not this drug class.
  89. The correct answer is B (Ductal adenocarcinoma of the pancreatic head obstructing the distal common bile duct and main pancreatic duct): Painless obstructive jaundice, weight loss, new onset diabetes, migratory venous thrombosis, and a hypodense head mass with dilation of both ducts is classic for pancreatic ductal adenocarcinoma. A distal cholangiocarcinoma would not produce a parenchymal mass with pancreatic duct dilation, and a stone would more likely cause pain and fever.
  90. The correct answer is D (Hydrochlorothiazide blocking the sodium chloride cotransporter in the distal convoluted tubule, which enhances calcium reabsorption): Thiazides block the sodium chloride cotransporter in the distal convoluted tubule, which lowers intracellular sodium and drives calcium reabsorption through the basolateral sodium calcium exchanger, so urinary calcium falls and blood pressure improves. Loop diuretics have the opposite effect on calcium and would worsen stone risk.
  91. The correct answer is D (Adolescent idiopathic scoliosis, a structural lateral spinal curve of unknown cause in teenagers): A persistent rib hump on forward bending that remains when seated shows a structural curve with vertebral rotation, and a normal neurologic and skin examination with equal leg lengths points to adolescent idiopathic scoliosis. Functional curves from leg length discrepancy correct on sitting, and congenital curves are typically recognized earlier.
  92. The correct answer is A (Unrestrained adipose lipolysis delivers fatty acids to liver, driving triglyceride synthesis and increased VLDL secretion): In insulin resistance, adipose tissue fails to suppress hormone-sensitive lipase, so free fatty acids flood the liver and drive triglyceride synthesis and VLDL secretion. This also lowers HDL through lipid exchange. Familial LDL receptor and apolipoprotein C-II defects are monogenic and do not fit a patient with central obesity and elevated glucose.
  93. The correct answer is D (Duloxetine, a serotonin and norepinephrine reuptake inhibitor that augments descending inhibition of pain pathways): Fibromyalgia reflects central sensitization with reduced descending pain inhibition, so drugs that raise serotonin and norepinephrine, such as duloxetine, have the best supporting evidence alongside exercise. Opioids and corticosteroids are not recommended, and NSAIDs add little. Methotrexate treats inflammatory arthritis, which is not present with normal inflammatory markers.
  94. The correct answer is C (Perfusion pressure of 22 mm Hg is below the usual threshold and supports fasciotomy): Perfusion pressure equals diastolic pressure minus compartment pressure, here 22 mm Hg, and a value under about 30 mm Hg indicates inadequate muscle perfusion. Adding the two pressures is a common arithmetic error. Delay risks irreversible necrosis, and arterial injury is not required, since distal pulses are often preserved.
  95. The correct answer is D (T5 to T9 paraspinal changes reflecting sympathetic outflow to the stomach and upper gastrointestinal tract): Sympathetic input to the stomach and upper gut arises from about T5 to T9, so an acute viscerosomatic reflex from gastritis produces warm, boggy paraspinal tissue there. The proximal colon maps lower, around T10 to T11.
  96. The correct answer is C (L1 to L2, supplying the distal large bowel through the lumbar splanchnic nerves): The distal colon, including the sigmoid segment, receives sympathetic input from L1 to L2, so tissue texture change there marks the relevant viscerosomatic reflex. The T10 to T11 range is tempting but serves the colon proximal to the resection. Sacral outflow is parasympathetic, whereas this treatment targets sympathetic tone.
  97. The correct answer is A (Reduced canal diameter during lumbar extension compressing nerve roots, relieved when the spine flexes): Neurogenic claudication from lumbar spinal stenosis worsens with extension and walking and improves with flexion, such as leaning on a cart or cycling. Vascular claudication improves with simple standing and is accompanied by diminished pulses. A herniated disc typically worsens with sitting.
  98. The correct answer is B (Nodular mesangial expansion with eosinophilic hyaline material and diffusely thickened glomerular basement membranes): Long standing diabetes with retinopathy and persistent albuminuria without active urine sediment indicates diabetic nephropathy. Hyperglycemia causes basement membrane thickening and mesangial expansion that forms the classic nodules of Kimmelstiel-Wilson disease. The other patterns belong to nephritic or nephrotic syndromes with different clinical clues.
  99. The correct answer is A (Hyperinsulinemia stimulating keratinocyte and fibroblast proliferation through insulin-like growth factor receptors in the skin): High circulating insulin binds insulin-like growth factor receptors on keratinocytes and dermal fibroblasts, causing papillomatosis and hyperkeratosis in flexural skin. Obesity with elevated fasting glucose points to insulin resistance. ACTH excess darkens skin diffusely and does not produce a velvety texture.
  100. The correct answer is E (Sodium glucose cotransporter 2 inhibitor, which promotes natriuresis and lowers cardiac filling pressures): This is heart failure with preserved ejection fraction, where sodium glucose cotransporter 2 inhibitors lower hospitalization and cardiovascular death in addition to diuretics for congestion. Rate slowing drugs and inotropes have no outcome benefit when systolic function is normal. The nitrate and hydralazine combination is validated in reduced ejection fraction.

Practice Test 10 Answer Key

  1. The correct answer is C (Use a qualified professional interpreter and permit the grandson to stay as support if she agrees): Qualified interpreters improve accuracy and safety and protect confidentiality, and a minor child should not interpret for sensitive or complex medical discussions. Bilingual staff without interpreter training can omit or distort content. The grandson can remain for support only if the patient wishes.
  2. The correct answer is E (Partial agonism, producing a submaximal effect even when every receptor is occupied): A partial agonist has lower intrinsic activity, so even with full receptor occupancy the response stays below that of a full agonist, which creates a ceiling on respiratory depression. A competitive antagonist would shift the curve but not lower the maximum. Low potency changes only the dose needed, not the maximal effect.
  3. The correct answer is A (Calculate food-specific attack rates among exposed and unexposed diners to test the leading hypothesis): The diagnosis is verified, cases are defined and counted, and the curve shows a point-source pattern, so the investigation now moves to testing the hypothesis. Comparing attack rates in those who ate each item with those who did not identifies the likely vehicle. Public communication follows, since discarded food leaves no ongoing exposure to interrupt.
  4. The correct answer is C (Allopurinol, a xanthine oxidase inhibitor linked to HLA-B*58:01 hypersensitivity in this ancestry): Mucosal erosions with epidermal detachment over 10 to 30 percent of the body surface describe Stevens-Johnson and toxic epidermal necrolysis overlap, and allopurinol in patients carrying HLA-B*58:01, especially of Han Chinese ancestry, is a classic cause. Febuxostat belongs to the same class but lacks this strong HLA association.
  5. The correct answer is E (Rifampin, which induces hepatic cytochrome P450 enzymes, accelerating estrogen metabolism and coloring secretions orange): Rifampin turns tears, urine, and sweat orange and is a potent inducer of cytochrome P450, which speeds clearance of estrogen and progestin and causes contraceptive failure. Streptomycin is tempting because of the long standing flora theory, but evidence supports enzyme induction by rifampin. Isoniazid, in contrast, inhibits these enzymes.
  6. The correct answer is E (Left posterior inferior cerebellar artery supplying the lateral medulla): Wallenberg lateral medullary syndrome results from occlusion of the posterior inferior cerebellar artery or the vertebral artery. Ipsilateral facial pain and temperature loss (spinal trigeminal nucleus), contralateral body loss (spinothalamic tract), Horner syndrome, dysphagia and hoarseness (nucleus ambiguus) and ataxia all fit. Anterior inferior cerebellar artery lesions add facial paralysis and deafness.
  7. The correct answer is C (REM sleep, a paradoxical stage with wake-like cortical activity driven by pontine cholinergic neurons): REM sleep combines low-voltage fast EEG activity, rapid eye movements, skeletal muscle atonia, vivid dreaming and irregular autonomic activity, and it is generated by cholinergic neurons of the pontine reticular formation. N3 shows delta waves with sleepwalking and growth hormone release, N2 shows spindles and K complexes, and relaxed wakefulness shows alpha rhythm with intact muscle tone.
  8. The correct answer is E (Fiber, fluids, and sitz baths with a topical agent that relaxes the internal sphincter): Acute fissures usually heal with stool softening, bulk, warm baths, and a topical calcium channel blocker or nitroglycerin that lowers sphincter pressure. Sphincterotomy is for chronic fissures that fail medical therapy. Banding and drainage address other anorectal problems.
  9. The correct answer is A (Intestinal atresia from vascular compromise of the exposed bowel, with no increased chromosomal risk): A paraumbilical defect beside a normally inserted cord with uncovered eviscerated bowel is gastroschisis, usually seen in young mothers. The exposed loops can twist or lose blood supply in utero, producing intestinal atresia. Chromosomal and cardiac associations belong to omphalocele, in which the midline herniated gut is covered by a membrane.
  10. The correct answer is E (Acute somatic dysfunction reflecting local edema, vasodilation, and early inflammatory congestion of the segmental soft tissues): Warm, moist, boggy, erythematous tissue after sudden injury is the classic texture of acute somatic dysfunction. Chronic dysfunction would feel cool, dry, and ropy, and the long-standing shoulder aching is a separate complaint that does not make the lumbar findings subacute.
  11. The correct answer is D (Send a throat culture to confirm or exclude group A streptococcal infection before treating): Fever, exudates, tender nodes and no cough give a high pretest probability of group A streptococcus. In children a negative rapid antigen test should be followed by a throat culture because the rapid test misses some cases, and treatment prevents rheumatic fever. Mononucleosis testing and imaging are not indicated by this presentation.
  12. The correct answer is D (S1 root, compressed by a posterolateral herniation of the L5 to S1 disc): A reduced ankle jerk, weak plantarflexion, and lateral foot numbness indicate S1 root compression, typically from a posterolateral L5 to S1 herniation that catches the traversing root. A normal patellar reflex argues against L3 or L4 involvement. L5 root compression would weaken dorsiflexion and great toe extension with a preserved ankle jerk.
  13. The correct answer is A (Acute tubular necrosis from medullary hypoxia and direct toxicity to renal tubular epithelium): A rise in creatinine 24 to 72 hours after contrast with muddy brown granular casts, a fractional excretion of sodium above 2 percent, and urine osmolality near that of plasma indicates acute tubular necrosis. Prerenal disease would give a low urine sodium and concentrated urine. Atheroembolism follows arterial catheterization, not intravenous contrast, and brings eosinophilia.
  14. The correct answer is A (Proliferation of both stromal and epithelial elements compressing ducts into slit-like spaces within loose myxoid stroma): A fibroadenoma is a benign biphasic lesion in which proliferating stroma and epithelium compress ducts into slit-like spaces. Its mobility, smooth margins, and lack of cyclic change separate it from fibrocystic change. A phyllodes tumor would show leaf-like architecture with hypercellular stroma and would usually grow quickly.
  15. The correct answer is E (Intravenous loop diuretic given early to relieve pulmonary congestion through venodilation and natriuresis): He is warm and wet with preserved perfusion, so rapid decongestion with an intravenous loop diuretic is first. Milrinone is reserved for low output states with poor perfusion. Starting a beta blocker during acute pulmonary edema can worsen failure, and a saline bolus adds to the congestion.
  16. The correct answer is C (Indirect positioning of ease at adjacent segments, with no direct force applied through the involved vertebra): A lytic metastasis makes the vertebra vulnerable to fracture, so thrust and forceful direct techniques are contraindicated at that segment. Gentle indirect positioning of adjacent regions can still influence sympathetic tone without loading the lesion. Muscle energy and articulatory methods are direct and still apply load across the involved segment.
  17. The correct answer is C (Magnetic resonance imaging, which detects early marrow edema and cortical stress reaction before plain films change): Focal bony tenderness with pain on hopping in a recruit suggests a tibial stress injury, and plain films are often normal for the first weeks. MRI detects marrow edema and periosteal reaction early without ionizing radiation. Repeating films within a day adds nothing, and densitometry does not evaluate a focal injury.
  18. The correct answer is E (T1 to T6, the sympathetic outflow levels supplying the bronchi and pulmonary vasculature): Sympathetic fibers to the bronchi and lungs leave the cord at about T1 to T6, so acute asthma produces reflex changes in the paraspinal tissues at those levels. The T5 to T9, T10 to L1, and L1 to L2 ranges belong to the stomach, kidney, and distal colon, and S2 to S4 is a parasympathetic pelvic outflow.
  19. The correct answer is D (Broad spectrum intravenous antibiotics with urgent endoscopic biliary decompression by ERCP): Fever, jaundice, pain, hypotension and confusion form Reynolds pentad, indicating severe suppurative cholangitis that needs prompt biliary drainage along with antibiotics. Antibiotics alone may suffice in mild disease, but shock demands decompression. Cholecystectomy treats the gallbladder, not the infected duct.
  20. The correct answer is E (Topical permethrin applied from the neck down and repeated after one week, with contacts treated): Scabies from Sarcoptes scabiei causes nocturnal pruritus and burrows in web spaces, and household contacts need simultaneous treatment even if asymptomatic. Permethrin 5 percent cream is first line, and lindane is reserved for failures because of neurotoxicity.
  21. The correct answer is A (Continuous positive airway pressure applied nightly to splint the collapsing pharyngeal airway): Moderate to severe obstructive sleep apnea with resistant hypertension is treated first with CPAP, which prevents pharyngeal collapse and can improve blood pressure. Sedative-hypnotics can worsen obstruction, and surgery is a later option. Oxygen alone or a stimulant leaves apneas and arousals untreated.
  22. The correct answer is C (Increased synaptic acetylcholine from cholinesterase inhibition enhancing vagal and gastrointestinal muscarinic activity): Cholinesterase inhibitors such as donepezil raise acetylcholine and increase vagal tone, producing bradycardia, syncope, nausea, and diarrhea through muscarinic stimulation. Memantine blocks NMDA receptors and rarely slows the heart. Catecholamine elevation would raise heart rate, not lower it.
  23. The correct answer is D (Low potassium, low sodium, and high calcium with a mild metabolic alkalosis): Thiazides block the sodium chloride cotransporter in the distal tubule, increasing distal sodium delivery and potassium loss, causing hypokalemia, hyponatremia, and alkalosis. They enhance calcium reabsorption, so calcium rises, unlike loop diuretics. The other patterns belong to loop, potassium sparing, carbonic anhydrase inhibitor, and renin angiotensin drugs.
  24. The correct answer is A (Absence of a peptidoglycan cell wall, which removes the target of beta-lactam antibiotics): Gradual onset, dry cough, interstitial infiltrates out of proportion to examination, and a Gram stain showing no organisms fit Mycoplasma pneumoniae. It lacks a cell wall, so amoxicillin has no peptidoglycan to disrupt, and it does not stain with the Gram method. A macrolide or doxycycline acts on the ribosome and is effective.
  25. The correct answer is C (Serum insulin-like growth factor 1 level, reflecting integrated growth hormone secretion over the preceding day): Growth hormone is released in pulses, so IGF-1, produced by the liver in response to sustained exposure, is the best screening test for acromegaly. A random growth hormone value can be normal or high by chance. Confirmation uses failure of growth hormone to suppress after a glucose load, and MRI comes after biochemical diagnosis.
  26. The correct answer is C (Intravenous procainamide, slowing conduction in the accessory pathway and prolonging its refractory period): Irregular wide-complex tachycardia with varying morphology in a patient with WPW indicates preexcited atrial fibrillation, in which AV nodal blockers can increase conduction down the accessory pathway and precipitate ventricular fibrillation. Procainamide slows accessory pathway conduction, so it is the appropriate drug in a stable patient. Digoxin even shortens accessory pathway refractoriness.
  27.
