The correct answer is A (Type I group dysfunction in neutral, sidebent left and rotated right (NSLRR)): A multisegment group curve with sidebending and rotation to opposite sides that does not change with flexion or extension is a type I neutral dysfunction. Because dysfunctions are named for the direction of freedom, it is sidebent left and rotated right. Type II is a single non-neutral segment with sidebending and rotation to the same side, and type III involves motion introduced in one plane modifying the others.
  28. The correct answer is A (Cobalamin deficiency from drug related malabsorption, which blocks both methionine synthase and methylmalonyl CoA mutase): Metformin and acid suppression both reduce cobalamin absorption. Macrocytic anemia, dorsal column signs, and elevation of both homocysteine and methylmalonic acid indicate cobalamin deficiency, since methylmalonyl CoA mutase needs it as well as methionine synthase. Folate deficiency raises homocysteine alone and does not cause the neurologic findings.
  29. The correct answer is B (Normal or low uptake at many lytic sites, since tracer needs osteoblastic activity that is minimal): Technetium-99m diphosphonate localizes where osteoblasts are forming new bone. Multiple myeloma lesions are purely lytic with suppressed osteoblast activity, so the bone scan is often falsely negative, which is why skeletal survey, MRI or PET is preferred. Prostate metastases, by contrast, are osteoblastic and produce hot uptake.
  30. The correct answer is C (False positives are tolerable because biopsy resolves them, and missed cases cost lives): When missed disease is fatal and curable while confirmatory biopsy is safe and cheap, the cost of a false positive is small and the cost of a false negative is large. A cutoff favoring sensitivity is therefore appropriate, and biopsy removes the false positives. Favoring specificity would sacrifice the sensitivity that protects patients, and positive predictive value is secondary here.
  31. The correct answer is D (Factitious disorder imposed on another, with fabricated illness in a dependent to gain attention): High insulin with suppressed C-peptide indicates exogenous insulin, so the illness is being produced deliberately in the child. When the motive is the caregiver's psychological need for the sick role by proxy, rather than a tangible reward, the diagnosis is factitious disorder imposed on another. Malingering requires an external incentive, which the stem excludes.
  32. The correct answer is A (Rib 7, the lowest rib, because its restriction prevents the entire group from exhaling): In an inhalation group the ribs are stuck in inhalation and cannot exhale, and the lowest rib is the key rib because it locks the group in that position. For an exhalation group the key rib is the highest one, which reverses this pattern.
  33. The correct answer is E (Muscle energy to the right innominate with the patient side-lying, avoiding prolonged prone positioning): A positive standing flexion test with a negative seated test places the restriction at the iliosacral joint, so an innominate somatic dysfunction is treated. Muscle energy is a gentle, patient-activated direct technique that works side-lying, avoiding the discomfort of prone positioning late in pregnancy. Thrust techniques add strain to ligaments loosened by relaxin, and uterine visceral work does not address this finding.
  34. Gentle indirect or soft tissue treatment addresses the muscle tenderness without stressing the unstable joint.

  36. Noncompetitive and irreversible inhibitors lower Vmax, and an activator would lower Km.
  37. Pectoralis minor serves ribs 3 to 5, latissimus dorsi serves ribs 10 and 11, quadratus lumborum serves rib 12, and the scalenes serve ribs 1 and 2, so each is matched to a different rib group.
  39. The correct answer is E (Clearance of 90 mL/min, calculated as urine concentration times flow divided by plasma concentration): Clearance equals urine concentration times urine flow divided by plasma concentration, so 120 times 1.5 divided by 2 gives 90 mL/min. Inulin is freely filtered and neither secreted nor reabsorbed, so its clearance equals filtration rate; the plasma creatinine value is irrelevant to this calculation.
  40. Acetaldehyde accumulates and causes flushing, tachycardia, and nausea. Lack of the first enzyme would instead leave ethanol unmetabolized and would not produce this acetaldehyde reaction.
  41. Alprostadil does the opposite and is used in ductal dependent lesions. Diuretics may ease symptoms but do not close the vessel.
  42. The correct answer is D (Reduce the maintenance dose in proportion to the reduced clearance, leaving the loading dose unchanged): Average steady-state concentration equals dosing rate divided by clearance, so lower clearance requires a proportionally lower maintenance rate. The loading dose depends on volume of distribution, which is largely unchanged by renal impairment. Monitoring alone would let the drug accumulate toward toxicity.

Long-acting beta agonist monotherapy is unsafe in asthma, and prophylactic antifungals are not standard.
  44. The correct answer is A (Reactive oxygen species damaging alveolar epithelium and capillary endothelium, causing diffuse alveolar damage and edema): Prolonged exposure to a high oxygen fraction generates superoxide and hydroxyl radicals that injure alveolar epithelium and endothelium, causing diffuse alveolar damage and noncardiogenic edema after about 24 to 72 hours or more. Absorption atelectasis happens earlier, and the sterile lavage and improving sepsis argue against infection.
  45.   47. Class IA and III drugs such as procainamide and amiodarone prolong the QT interval further and can worsen it. Potassium should also be repleted, but that alone is not the best immediate step.

The calcaneofibular tears with more severe inversion, and the deltoid is injured by eversion instead.

  52. Extension reverses this pattern with internal rotation of paired bones and a narrower, longer head. The sphenoid and occiput move in the same phase together.
  53. The correct answer is C (Atrioventricular node, whose slow calcium-dependent depolarization provides the pause between atrial and ventricular systole): AV nodal cells are small, have few gap junctions and depend on slow calcium channel upstrokes, so the AV node produces most of the PR interval and allows atrial emptying before ventricular systole. Purkinje fibers conduct the fastest in the heart, so they cannot account for the delay.
  54. The correct answer is E (Inhibition of carbonic anhydrase in proximal tubule cells, which reduces hydrogen secretion and bicarbonate reabsorption): Acetazolamide inhibits carbonic anhydrase in proximal tubule cells, so less hydrogen is secreted and less bicarbonate is reabsorbed. The resulting mild metabolic acidosis drives ventilation and offsets the respiratory alkalosis of altitude. Loop and thiazide diuretics instead tend to cause contraction alkalosis.
  55. The correct answer is C (Gout from monosodium urate deposition, promoted by diuretic therapy and impaired renal urate excretion): Needle-shaped crystals that are yellow when parallel to the compensator axis are negatively birefringent, which identifies monosodium urate and therefore gout. Thiazide diuretics and kidney disease raise urate. Calcium pyrophosphate crystals are rhomboid and positively birefringent, and an osteoarthritic effusion would have a far lower cell count.

The bony changes are reactive. Pannus and autoantibodies characterize rheumatoid arthritis, which spares the distal interphalangeal joints and causes prolonged morning stiffness.
  59.   66. A narrow-margin full-thickness excisional biopsy keeps the whole lesion intact so Breslow thickness can be measured accurately, which guides later wide excision.   69. The odds ratio can be calculated from the exposure odds and approximates relative risk for rare diseases. Attributable risk, absolute risk reduction and number needed to treat all require incidence.

  71.   72.   95. The correct answer is E (High sensitivity, since few diseased patients are missed when the assay returns normal): A test used to exclude a dangerous disease must rarely miss it, which requires high sensitivity so that a normal result carries strong rule-out value. High specificity helps confirm disease rather than exclude it, and positive predictive value actually falls when disease is rare. Cost and speed matter practically but do not determine how reliably a result excludes disease.
